ADR-030 keystone latency observed at ~2-3s (Kafka round-trip; single-threaded per tenant via processWithIdempotency); synthetic Platform Admin (`admin@`) cannot administer medication because they have no `hr_employees`row (Step 6 deliberately refuses such callers — clinical record). **REVIEW-CYCLE10 BLOCKING fix (closeout commit):** the reviewer flagged that`GET /health/medications/:id/administrations`was gated on`hlt-001:read`(which guardians hold) with the service layer explicitly allowing`actor.personType === 'GUARDIAN'`, exposing the full clinical dose-by-dose log (administered_at / dose given / missed_reason / parent_notified / administering staff name). The handoff's vertical-slice privacy contract said "no medication administration log" on the parent surface and the Step 9 parent UI honoured that, but the API endpoint did not. Fix: controller re-gated to `@RequirePermission('hlt-002:read')`(which guardians do NOT hold); service`listForMedication`removes the GUARDIAN branch entirely, replacing it with`if (!(await this.records.hasNurseScope(actor)))`so admins/nurses reach it and everyone else 403s; ApiOperation summary rewritten; CAT script Scenario 7 gains a new S7.I sub-check verifying parent denial. **Live verified on`tenant_demo`2026-05-04 (6 scenarios all green):** R1 admin GET 200 / R2 counsellor GET 200 / **R3 parent GET 403** (the BLOCKING fix) / R4 teacher GET 403 / R5 student GET 403 / R6 parent CAN still read`/students/<maya>/medications`200 with`prescribingPhysician=null`(the parent-safe summary surface is preserved). **MAJOR follow-ups (deferred to Wave 2 Phase 2):** scheduled-dose duplicate logging (no UNIQUE/advisory lock on`(medication, schedule_entry, date)`— reviewer accepts as non-blocking; fix pattern = Cycle 6 InvoiceService.generateFromSchedule advisory lock + in-tx existence check OR partial UNIQUE on`(schedule_entry_id, administered_at::date) WHERE was_missed=false`); Nurse / Health Office / Counsellor role split (Staff role currently grants all 5 HLT codes — fine for demo, narrow before pilot); IEP parent visibility (API surfaces full plan to GUARDIAN per IDEA/504 statute; UI hides it on the summary card — locked product decision documented in `REVIEW-CYCLE10-CHATGPT.md`). See `REVIEW-CYCLE10-CHATGPT.md`for the triage table + before/after code + verification trail. Tagged`cycle10-approved` on the closeout commit.

Cycle 9 is now clean from the review perspective. The privacy blocker is closed, and the remaining item is a role-design hardening task rather than a cycle blocker."_ Reviewer cache-busted the four affected files in code on Round 2 and confirmed each fix: parent feedback leak fixed via `canSeeFeedback()` short-circuit; feedback endpoint Swagger doc fixed; feedback request duplicate race fixed via tenant tx + 23505 catch; BIP activation race documented as fixed with no contrary code signal. The closeout fix commit lands the BLOCKING fix + 3 of the 4 MAJORs (MAJOR 4 Counsellor role split appropriately moves to Phase 2 backlog per the reviewer's gate decision — _"I would not block the cycle on these if the feedback privacy leak is corrected. They should become tracked follow-ups."_). Round 1 reviewer scorecard: 9 PASS / 4 DEVIATION-FOLLOW-UP / 1 VIOLATION (the BLOCKING). REVIEW-CYCLE9 fixes (2026-05-04):** (BLOCKING — privacy) `GET /behavior-plans/:id/feedback` was gated only by `beh-002:read`, calling `plans.getById()` to validate plan visibility then returning every `svc_bip_teacher_feedback` row unfiltered. Parents who hold `BEH-002:read` (granted in Step 9 for the per-child Behaviour tab) could read the rows the main plan service intentionally strips via `canSeeFeedback()` — bypass via the dedicated endpoint. Fix: `BehaviorPlanService.canSeeFeedback(actor)` is now public and excludes both GUARDIAN and STUDENT; `FeedbackService.listForPlan()` calls it after the plan-visibility check and returns `[]` if the actor cannot see feedback. Live verified on `tenant_demo` against Maya's seeded BIP — parent (David Chen) `count=0`, counsellor (Hayes) / admin (Sarah) / teacher (Rivera, own-class scope) `count=1`, student (Maya) `403`. (MAJOR 2 — bug) `BehaviorPlanService.activate()` keeps the partial-UNIQUE pre-flight surfacing the conflicting plan id, and now wraps the UPDATE in a try/catch on `isUniqueViolation()` so a concurrent activation race surfaces the same friendly 400 instead of a raw SQLSTATE 23505. Helper added to the file mirroring `discipline/action.service.ts` + `profile/profile.service.ts`. (MAJOR 3 — bug) `FeedbackService.requestFeedback()` now runs the partial-UNIQUE pre-check + INSERT inside one `executeInTenantTransaction` (was two separate read+write helpers) so the read sees the snapshot the INSERT writes against, plus a try/catch on `isUniqueViolation()` for the race-loser path. Both pre-flight branches verified live on `tenant_demo` (`Student already has an ACTIVE BIP plan (<id>). Expire that plan before activating a new one.` and `A pending feedback request already exists for this teacher on this plan (<id>). Wait for the teacher to submit before opening another request.`). (MAJOR 5 — doc) Swagger summary on `GET /behavior-plans/:id/feedback` rewritten to "Staff/counsellor/admin only — guardians and students always receive an empty array. Parent BIP summaries never include teacher feedback (REVIEW-CYCLE9 BLOCKING)." MAJOR 4 (Counsellor role split — `BEH-002:write` is currently granted to the generic Staff role which the seed describes as covering VPs / counsellors / admin assistants; service treats `beh-002:write` as counsellor scope, mechanically correct but broader than the domain language) is carried to the Wave 2 Phase 2 punch list — introducing a distinct Counsellor role is an architectural change that should land before real schools onboard. Strong passes (Round 1, unchanged): module registration; discipline visibility + adminNotes strip; incident lifecycle row locking with `resolved_chk` lockstep; discipline schema (4 base tables / 5 intra-tenant FKs / 0 cross-schema FKs); admin-only action assignment; behaviour-plan schema invariants (partial UNIQUEs + non-empty target\*behaviors); behaviour-plan parent row scope on the main payload (the bug was only the separate feedback endpoint, now fixed); Cycle 7 TaskWorker reliability fix preserved; behaviour notifications follow claim-after-success. Cycle 9 is the **first cycle of Wave 2 (Student Services)** and ships M20 SIS Discipline (4 tables — deferred from Cycle 1 since they were not needed for the attendance vertical slice) plus M27 Behaviour Plans (3 tables — first Student Services tables in the system, `svc*`prefix). The vertical slice: teacher reports an incident "Verbal altercation in the hallway" under Disrespect (MEDIUM) → admin reviews → admin assigns Detention with`requires*parent_notification=true`→ parent David Chen receives IN\*APP notification → admin resolves → counsellor creates a BIP linked to the incident with target/replacement behaviours + 3 measurable goals → counsellor requests teacher feedback on strategy effectiveness → teacher submits feedback → counsellor updates goal progress. This is the first cycle to involve **sensitive student conduct data** — row-level visibility is strict (teachers see incidents they reported or that involve students in their classes; parents see their own children's incidents excluding`admin_notes`; students never see discipline records directly; admins/counsellors see all). Plan at `docs/campusos-cycle9-implementation-plan.html`; handoff at `HANDOFF-CYCLE9.md`. **Step 1 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/030_sis_discipline.sql`** with 4 logical base tables: `sis_discipline_categories`(4-value`severity`CHECK LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL;`is_active`flag for soft deactivation; UNIQUE(school_id, name); INDEX(school_id, is_active) for the active-list hot path),`sis_discipline_action_types` (`requires_parent_notification BOOLEAN DEFAULT false`flags actions that fire the parent-notification path in the Step 4 ActionService;`is_active`for soft deactivation; UNIQUE(school_id, name)),`sis_discipline_incidents` (`student_id`NOT NULL FK to`sis_students(id)`ON DELETE CASCADE — the conservative privacy choice when a student is removed;`reported_by`FK to`hr_employees(id)`ON DELETE SET NULL — audit trail survives a teacher leaving;`category_id`NOT NULL FK to`sis_discipline_categories(id)`NO ACTION — admin must deactivate via`is_active=false`rather than hard-delete; 3-value`status`CHECK OPEN/UNDER_REVIEW/RESOLVED with multi-column`resolved_chk`keeping`resolved_by`+`resolved_at`in lockstep with status — working states require both NULL, RESOLVED requires both NOT NULL;`admin_notes`is internal and the Step 4 row-scope filter strips it from the parent payload; INDEX(student_id, incident_date DESC) for the student-history hot path + partial INDEX(school_id, status) WHERE status != 'RESOLVED' for the admin queue),`sis_discipline_actions`(FK to`sis_discipline_incidents(id)`ON DELETE CASCADE — a consequence has no meaning without its incident; FK to`sis_discipline_action_types(id)`NO ACTION; UNIQUE(incident_id, action_type_id) — admins layer different consequence types rather than stacking duplicates; multi-column`dates_chk`enforcing`end_date >= start_date`only when both are set, either side null accepted for verbal warnings or open-ended consequences). **5 new intra-tenant DB-enforced FKs** (1 CASCADE on incidents → students; 1 SET NULL on incidents → hr_employees; 1 NO ACTION on incidents → categories; 1 CASCADE on actions → incidents; 1 NO ACTION on actions → action_types), 0 cross-schema FKs. **Tenant logical base table count after Step 1: 132 → 136**. **Live verification on`tenant_demo`(single BEGIN…ROLLBACK with savepoints, 17 assertions all green):** happy-path 4-insert across all 4 tables; severity_chk + dates_chk reject bogus values; resolved_chk fires on all 4 mismatch directions (OPEN-with-resolved_at; RESOLVED-without-resolved_at; RESOLVED-without-resolved_by; the BOGUS-status row also gets caught by the resolved_chk's status-list filter rather than the standalone status_chk — same row-rejection outcome, just a different constraint name in the error; Cycles 5/8 documented this same observation); lifecycle UPDATE OPEN→RESOLVED with both fields populated in the same UPDATE accepted; all 3 UNIQUE constraints reject duplicates ((school, name) on categories + action_types; (incident_id, action_type_id) on actions); 2 FK rejection paths fire on bogus student_id and bogus category_id; NO ACTION on category delete with referencing incident rejected; CASCADE on incident delete drops linked actions (1 → 0); NO ACTION on action_type delete with referencing action rejected. **All 5 FK delete actions confirmed via`pg_constraint.confdeltype`** catalog readout (CASCADE 'c' / SET NULL 'n' / NO ACTION 'a' / CASCADE 'c' / NO ACTION 'a'). 4-value`progress`CHECK NOT_STARTED/IN_PROGRESS/MET/NOT_MET),`svc_bip_teacher_feedback`(CASCADE on plan; nullable 4-value`overall_effectiveness`CHECK NOT_EFFECTIVE/SOMEWHAT_EFFECTIVE/EFFECTIVE/VERY_EFFECTIVE — NULL accepted while pending; The prior comparison was against `assigneeId` (an `hr_employees.id`) — category mismatch, never matched, so the self-notification suppression path was dead. Verified live: Sarah submits + resolves her own ticket → wire envelope shows `resolvedByAccountId === requesterId`; queue contains 0 `ticket.resolved` rows for her id. (Follow-up 3 — bug) `ProblemService.patch` now opens `executeInTenantTransaction`, locks the row + reads existing `root_cause` / `assigned_to_id` / `vendor_id` atomically, computes the post-patch effective shape, and rejects KNOWN_ERROR without effective rootCause + the assignee/vendor mutex + bogus FK targets — all with friendly 400s before the UPDATE fires. The DB CHECK is the schema-side belt-and-braces. Verified live with 5 scenarios. (Follow-up 4 — bug) `TicketService.create` adds an existence check on `locationId` against `sch_rooms` inside the existing tenant context. Bogus UUID returns `400 "locationId does not match a room in this school"`; real Room 101 id accepted cleanly. **Architectural follow-ups carried to Phase 2:** (Follow-up 1) Permission model rename or split — `IT-001` is functioning as the de-facto helpdesk function across IT/Facilities/HR-support tickets but the name is IT-only. Decision deferred until the M65 Facilities cycle in Wave 2. (Follow-up 5) Category uniqueness — `tkt_categories` UNIQUE(school_id, name) is global across the tree; switch to per-parent uniqueness when real-world collisions appear. **Four accepted deviations move to Wave 2 Phase 2 punch list:** consumer tenant routing trusts `tenant-subdomain` header; `KafkaProducerService.emit()` is best-effort (no outbox); `TenantResolverMiddleware` accepts `X-Tenant-Subdomain` in production; `PermissionGuard` needs a platform-scope auth path before v11 CRM/OPS modules. See `REVIEW-CYCLE8-CHATGPT.md` for the triage table and end-to-end verification trail.** This is the final cycle of Wave 1 and ships M60 Service Tickets — a shared reactive ticketing engine that IT, facilities, and HR support all use (NOT a facilities management system; that is M65, future cycle). The vertical slice: teacher submits "Projector not working in Room 101" under IT → Hardware → auto-assigned to IT admin via subcategory rules → SLA clock starts (HIGH priority, 4h response / 24h resolution) → admin comments, reassigns to vendor, resolves → requester notified → ticket auto-closes after 48h. Plan at `docs/campusos-cycle8-implementation-plan.html`; handoff at `HANDOFF-CYCLE8.md`. **Step 1 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/028_tkt_categories_sla_vendors.sql`** with 4 logical base tables for the M60 configuration layer: `tkt_categories` (hierarchical with self-FK; UNIQUE(school\*id, name) across the full tree; self-FK uses NO ACTION so admins must move/deactivate children before deleting a parent), `tkt_subcategories` (CASCADE on category; `default_assignee_id` FK to `hr_employees(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` so the seeded admin leaving the school does not break auto-assignment; optional `auto_assign_to_role` token resolved at submission time the same way Cycle 7's WorkflowEngineService resolves a ROLE-typed approver; UNIQUE(category_id, name)), `tkt_sla_policies` (priority 4-value CHECK LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL; `response_hours > 0` + `resolution_hours > 0` CHECKs plus a multi-column `order_chk` enforcing `resolution_hours >= response_hours` to prevent a misconfigured policy where the ticket is breached on resolution before it can be breached on response; UNIQUE(school, category, priority); CASCADE on category since SLA rows are meaningless without their category — the Step 4 SlaService computes breach status from `tkt_tickets.created_at` and `now()` against this table, the clock is not stored as a countdown), `tkt_vendors` (9-value `vendor_type` CHECK IT_REPAIR/FACILITIES_MAINTENANCE/CLEANING/ELECTRICAL/PLUMBING/HVAC/SECURITY/GROUNDS/OTHER; `is_preferred` flag drives the Step 8 admin assignment dropdown sort; UNIQUE(school, vendor_name); partial INDEX on `(school, is_preferred) WHERE is_preferred=true` for the preferred-first hot path). 4 new intra-tenant DB-enforced FKs (1 self-FK NO ACTION on `tkt_categories.parent_category_id`; 2 CASCADE — `subcategories.category_id` and `sla_policies.category_id`; 1 SET NULL — `subcategories.default_assignee_id` to `hr_employees`), 0 cross-schema FKs. **Tenant logical base table count after Step 1: 125** (the live database showed 121 pre-Cycle-8, not 120 as the Cycle 7 narrative reported — off-by-one drift originated when Cycle 6.1's `sis_student_demographics` table was double-counted in the cycle text; real chain is 106 → 107 (Cycle 6.1 Step 2) → 108 → 109 (Phase 2 polish) → 115 (Cycle 7 Step 1) → 121 (Cycle 7 Step 2) → 125 (Cycle 8 Step 1); see `HANDOFF-CYCLE8.md` Step 1 for the full math). **Live verification on `tenant_demo` (15 assertions, 14 verified empirically + 1 verified via `pg_constraint` catalog inspection):** happy-path 5-insert across all 4 tables; vendor_type / priority / response_chk / order_chk all fire on bogus values; all 4 UNIQUE constraints reject duplicates; parent NO ACTION refuses delete of a category with children; bogus FKs on `subcategories.category_id` and `subcategories.default_assignee_id` rejected; CASCADE drops leaves when their parent category is deleted; CASCADE drops SLA rows when their category is deleted; SET NULL on `default_assignee_id` verified via `pg_constraint.confdeltype='n'` rather than empirically (creating a temp `hr_employees` row requires unrelated NOT NULL fixture work that the catalog readout makes unnecessary). All 4 FK delete actions confirmed via `pg_constraint` (NO ACTION / CASCADE / CASCADE / SET NULL). Splitter `;`-in-string trap not tripped — third cycle in a row to clear the splitter trap on first attempt. Idempotent re-provision verified (zero new applies on second run). Both `tenant_demo` and `tenant_test` provisioned cleanly. **Step 2 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/029_tkt_tickets_and_activity.sql`** with 7 logical base tables for the M60 operational layer: `tkt_tickets` (priority 4-value CHECK + status 7-value CHECK OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/VENDOR_ASSIGNED/PENDING_REQUESTER/RESOLVED/CLOSED/CANCELLED + three multi-column CHECKs — `assignee_or_vendor_chk` enforcing the internal-OR-vendor mutex, `vendor_pair_chk` keeping `vendor_id` and `vendor_assigned_at` all-set or all-null together, and `resolved_chk` keeping `resolved_at` and `closed_at` in lockstep with status across the 4 lifecycle phases (working ⇒ both NULL; RESOLVED ⇒ resolved_at NOT NULL only; CLOSED ⇒ both NOT NULL; CANCELLED ⇒ closed_at NOT NULL with resolved_at NULL since cancellation ends without resolution); 4 indexes — `(school_id, status, created_at DESC)` admin-queue hot path + partial `(assignee_id, status)` + partial `(vendor_id, status)` + `(requester_id, created_at DESC)`; soft `requester_id` and soft `location_id` per ADR-001/020 — the location ref to `sch_rooms` is kept soft so a ticket survives a Cycle 5 room being retired), `tkt_ticket_comments` (CASCADE on ticket; `is_internal` flag distinguishes staff-only notes from comments the requester sees), `tkt_ticket_attachments` (CASCADE; signed-S3-URL pattern matching `hr_employee_documents` from Cycle 4; non-negative `file_size_bytes` CHECK), `tkt_ticket_tags` (CASCADE; UNIQUE(ticket_id, tag) prevents duplicate tagging), `tkt_ticket_activity` (CASCADE; **IMMUTABLE audit log per ADR-010 — service-side discipline, no DB trigger so emergency operator action remains possible**; 7-value `activity_type` CHECK STATUS_CHANGE/REASSIGNMENT/COMMENT/ATTACHMENT/ESCALATION/VENDOR_ASSIGNMENT/SLA_BREACH; nullable `actor_id` so system-driven entries like SLA_BREACH from a future cron worker can land without an actor; `metadata JSONB DEFAULT '{}'` carries before/after for status changes and reassignments), `tkt_problems` (4-value status CHECK OPEN/INVESTIGATING/KNOWN_ERROR/RESOLVED; multi-column `assigned_or_vendor_chk` mirroring the ticket mutex; multi-column `resolved_chk` enforcing KNOWN_ERROR ⇒ root_cause NOT NULL, RESOLVED ⇒ root_cause + resolution + resolved_at all NOT NULL; assigned/vendor FKs SET NULL; category FK NO ACTION), `tkt_problem_tickets` (link table; UNIQUE(problem_id, ticket_id); double-CASCADE since the link has no meaning without either side). 14 new intra-tenant DB-enforced FKs (5 on tkt_tickets — category NO ACTION + subcategory NO ACTION + assignee SET NULL + vendor SET NULL + sla_policy SET NULL — preserving historical tickets when configuration is retired; 4 CASCADE on the operational children — comments + attachments + tags + activity; 3 on tkt_problems — category NO ACTION + assigned SET NULL + vendor SET NULL; 2 CASCADE on tkt_problem_tickets), 0 cross-schema FKs. **Tenant logical base table count after Step 2: 132** (was 125; +7 logical tkt\*\_ tables). Cycle 8 running tally: 11 tkt\_\_ tables. **Live verification on `tenant_demo` (single BEGIN…ROLLBACK with savepoints, 29 assertions all green):** happy-path multi-insert across all 7 tables; status_chk + priority_chk + assignee_or_vendor_chk + vendor_pair_chk both directions + resolved_chk all 4 branches; lifecycle round-trip OPEN→IN_PROGRESS→RESOLVED→CLOSED with timestamps landing in the same UPDATE; comment + activity inserts including a SLA_BREACH row with NULL actor_id (system-row pattern); activity_type CHECK rejects BOGUS; tag UNIQUE rejects duplicate; attachment size_chk rejects -1; NO ACTION on subcategory delete with referencing ticket; CASCADE on ticket delete drops 2 comments + 2 activity + 1 tag in one statement; tkt_problems status + resolved_chk + assigned_or_vendor_chk all fire on bogus values; tkt_problem_tickets UNIQUE rejects duplicate pair; CASCADE on problem delete and on ticket delete both drop link rows; bogus FKs on category_id and ticket_id rejected. **All 14 FK delete actions confirmed via `pg_constraint.confdeltype`** catalog readout (NO ACTION 'a' / CASCADE 'c' / SET NULL 'n'). **Constraint observation:** when a multi-column CHECK is structured as a multi-branch OR (the resolved_chk pattern from Cycles 5–7 + this Step), Postgres reports the OR-clause name as the failing constraint when a BOGUS status doesn't match any branch — same row-rejection outcome, just a different constraint name in the error than the standalone status_chk; Cycle 5 documented this for `sch_coverage_requests.assignment_chk` vs `status_chk`. Splitter `;`-in-string trap not tripped — fourth migration in a row to clear it on first attempt. Idempotent re-provision verified. Both `tenant_demo` and `tenant_test` applied cleanly. **Step 3 lands `packages/database/src/seed-tickets.ts`** (idempotent, gated on `tkt_categories` row count for the demo school) wired into `package.json` as `seed:tickets`. Seven sections seeded on `tenant_demo` (test tenant stays empty by convention): (A) **3 top-level `tkt_categories`** — IT (icon `computer`) / Facilities (icon `wrench`) / HR Support (icon `people`); (B) **11 `tkt_subcategories`** — 4 IT (Hardware / Software / Network / Account Access) + 5 Facilities (Electrical / Plumbing / HVAC / Cleaning / Furniture) + 2 HR Support (Payroll Question / Benefits Question); IT/Hardware exercises the direct `default_assignee_id` path with Sarah Mitchell as the IT admin stand-in (since the `admin@` Platform Admin persona is intentionally NOT bridged to `hr_employees` per the Cycle 4 Step 0 design), and Facilities/Electrical exercises the role-resolution path with `auto_assign_to_role='SCHOOL_ADMIN'` — the other 9 leaves land tickets in the admin queue unassigned by default; (C) **12 SLA policies** — 3 categories × 4 priorities, identical shape across all 3 categories per the demo: CRITICAL 1h/4h, HIGH 2h/8h, MEDIUM 4h/24h, LOW 8h/72h (live verification confirmed all 12 rows in the expected shape); (D) **2 vendors** — Springfield IT Solutions (IT_REPAIR, `is_preferred=true`, contact Patricia Nguyen) and Lincoln Maintenance Co (FACILITIES_MAINTENANCE, contact Greg Owens); (E) **5 sample tickets** covering 5 of the 7 lifecycle states (PENDING_REQUESTER and CANCELLED deliberately omitted — they require the Step 4 service to drive transitions): T1 "Projector not working in Room 101" IT/Hardware HIGH OPEN assigned to Mitchell; T2 "Leaking faucet in staff bathroom" Facilities/Plumbing MEDIUM IN_PROGRESS assigned to Mitchell with `first_response_at` populated; T3 "Can't access gradebook" IT/Software HIGH RESOLVED with `first_response_at`+`resolved_at` populated; T4 "Light out in hallway B" Facilities/Electrical LOW VENDOR_ASSIGNED to Lincoln Maintenance with `vendor_reference='WO-2026-0451'`+`vendor_assigned_at`; T5 "Payroll date question" HR Support/Payroll LOW CLOSED with `first_response_at`+`resolved_at`+`closed_at` populated end-to-end (passes the multi-column `resolved_chk` lifecycle invariant); requesters span Rivera/Park/Hayes so the demo exercises the row-scope filter the Step 4 service will apply; **3 sample comments** (1 public on T1 from Rivera, 1 internal on T2 from Mitchell with `is_internal=true`, 1 resolution on T3 from Mitchell); **8 activity rows** tracing lifecycle transitions across the 5 tickets (T1=1 COMMENT, T2=STATUS_CHANGE+COMMENT, T3=STATUS_CHANGE+COMMENT, T4=VENDOR_ASSIGNMENT+REASSIGNMENT, T5=STATUS_CHANGE roll-up); (F) **1 problem** "Network switch failure in Building A" status=INVESTIGATING assigned to Mitchell + 2 link rows tying T1 (Projector) and T3 (Gradebook access) — the Step 5 ProblemService.resolve admin endpoint will batch-resolve linked tickets in a future polish pass; (G) **1 auto-task rule** on `tkt.ticket.assigned` with `target_role=NULL` (relies on the TaskWorker's `payload.recipientAccountId / accountId` fallback so the Step 4 TicketService can map the assignee's `hr_employees.id` to `platform_users.id` before emitting), `priority='HIGH'`, `task_category='ADMINISTRATIVE'`, `due_offset_hours=24`, title template `Resolve ticket: {ticket_title}`, description template `SLA: {resolution_hours}h. Priority: {priority}.`, plus 1 `tsk_auto_task_actions` row CREATE_TASK sort_order=0. **`seed-iam.ts` updated:** Teacher and Staff each gain `IT-001:read+write` (the catalogue's existing entry for Helpdesk Tickets — `IT-001` and `FAC-001` were already in `permissions.json` waiting for Cycle 8 to use them); School Admin / Platform Admin already hold both `IT-001:admin` and `FAC-001:admin` via the `everyFunction: ['read','write','admin']` grant. Per the plan, `FAC-001` read/write is **not** extended to non-admin staff this cycle — `IT-001` is the umbrella code the Step 4 TicketService will gate on for all ticket categories. Cache rebuild reports 7 account-scope pairs — admin/principal 447 (unchanged), teacher 40 (was 38), student 19 (unchanged), parent 19 (unchanged), vp/counsellor 20 (was 18). Catalogue total stays at **447 functions × 3 tiers** (no new entries). **Live counts on `tenant_demo` after seed:** categories=3, subcategories=11 (1 with default_assignee + 1 with auto_assign_to_role), sla_policies=12, vendors=2 (1 preferred), tickets=5 (1 each across OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/RESOLVED/VENDOR_ASSIGNED/CLOSED), comments=3 (1 internal), activity=8, problems=1, problem_tickets=2, auto_task_rules_tkt=1, auto_task_actions_tkt=1. Idempotent re-run logs `tkt_categories already populated for demo school — skipping` with no INSERTs. `tenant_test` stays empty. **Step 4 lands `apps/api/src/tickets/`** with 4 services + 4 controllers + DTOs + TicketsModule wired into AppModule between WorkflowsModule and the global guards. **18 endpoints** total (5 categories under `it-001:read`/`admin` — list with subcategories inlined / create / patch / create-subcategory / patch-subcategory; 2 SLA — list under `it-001:read`, upsert under `it-001:admin`; 3 vendors — list / create / patch with preferred-first sort; 8 tickets — `GET /tickets` + `GET /tickets/:id` row-scoped to admin OR requester OR assignee, `POST /tickets` with auto-assignment chain, `PATCH /:id/{assign,assign-vendor,resolve,close,reopen,cancel}` all using locked-row `executeInTenantTransaction` + `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` per the convention). 3 Kafka emit topics — `tkt.ticket.submitted`, `tkt.ticket.assigned`, `tkt.ticket.resolved`. **Auto-assignment chain at submission**: subcategory.default_assignee_id (FK to hr_employees) → auto_assign_to_role token resolved via the same `roleTokenToName` helper the WorkflowEngineService uses for ROLE-typed approvers, then bridged through iam_person to land an hr_employees.id (returns null when the role only resolves to `admin@` since that persona is not bridged per Cycle 4 Step 0 — ticket lands unassigned with a LOG line) → admin queue unassigned. When auto-assignment lands an internal employee, status flips OPEN→IN_PROGRESS and `first_response_at` populates (system acknowledges on the assignee's behalf). **SLA auto-link**: `SlaService.lookupPolicyId(categoryId, priority)` denormalises the matching policy id onto the ticket; the clock is computed not stored — `SlaService.computeSnapshot()` returns `responseHoursRemaining` / `resolutionHoursRemaining` (negative = breached) from the timestamps at read time. **Locked-row transitions** for assign / assign-vendor / resolve / close / reopen / cancel; the schema's multi-column `resolved_chk` invariant means CLOSED requires prior RESOLVED (close-after-resolve) and CANCELLED keeps `resolved_at` NULL (cancellation ends without resolution); reopen clears `resolved_at` to flip back to OPEN. Every transition writes a `tkt_ticket_activity` row via the private `recordActivity()` helper (Step 5 will extract this into `ActivityService`). **TaskWorker side-fix:** `pickSourceRefId(payload)` in `apps/api/src/tasks/task.worker.ts` extended to recognise `sourceRefId` / `source_ref_id` (universal escape hatch) + `ticketId` / `ticket_id` (Cycle 8 emitter convention) — the existing per-domain candidate list was specific to assignment / grade / leave-request shapes and didn't cover the new ticket emit. Live verified end-to-end on `tenant_demo`: teacher Rivera POSTs IT/Hardware HIGH ticket → response shows assigneeName=Sarah Mitchell + status=IN_PROGRESS + slaPolicyId+ firstResponseAt populated + sla.responseHours=2/resolutionHours=8 → both ADR-057 envelopes captured live (`dev.tkt.ticket.submitted` + `dev.tkt.ticket.assigned` with `accountId`/`recipientAccountId`/`ticket_title`/`resolution_hours: 8`/`priority: HIGH`/`sourceRefId` populated) → Cycle 7 TaskWorker fires within ~3s creating an AUTO/ADMINISTRATIVE task on Sarah's list titled `Resolve ticket: Smoke ticket — Hardware` with `source_ref_id` = ticket id (after the worker fix); admin GET sees all 6 (5 seed + 1 smoke); teacher GET sees 4 (own row-scope filters out Park's T2 and Hayes's T4); admin PATCH /assign-vendor lands status=VENDOR_ASSIGNED + assigneeId cleared + vendorName=Springfield IT Solutions + vendorReference=WO-SMOKE-01; admin PATCH /resolve sets resolved_at + lands the resolution note as a public comment in the same tx; requester PATCH /close lands status=CLOSED+closedAt; 7 permission denials all behave correctly (student GET /tickets 403, parent POST 403, teacher POST /ticket-categories 403 admin-only, teacher POST /ticket-vendors 403, teacher PATCH /:id/assign 403, parent GET /tickets/:id 403 at gate, teacher GET /tickets/:T2_id 404 row-scope on Park's ticket). One smoke iteration issue caught (test-data only): pasting the category UUID from `psql -tA -c "SET …; SELECT …"` returned the SET command tag concatenated to the id; switched to schema-qualified queries (`SELECT id FROM tenant_demo.tkt_categories WHERE name='IT'`) with no leading SET, matching the prior-cycle convention. Smoke residue cleaned (DELETE 2 AUTO tasks + 2 smoke tickets via CASCADE). Build clean, all 18 routes mapped on boot. **Step 5 lands `apps/api/src/tickets/activity.service.ts` + `comment.service.ts` + `problem.service.ts` + matching controllers** — total cycle endpoint count grows to **27** (18 from Step 4 + 9 — 1 activity read + 2 comments + 6 problems). **ActivityService** hoists Step 4's private `recordActivity()` into a shared writer (`record(tx, ticketId, actorId, type, metadata)`) that CommentService and ProblemService both call through; the read path `GET /tickets/:id/activity` is row-scoped (admin OR requester OR assignee, else 404 don't-leak-existence). **CommentService** ships visibility-filter (admin sees all; assignee sees all; requester sees `is_internal=false` only; non-participant 404), POST runs everything in one tenant tx — INSERT comment + first-staff-comment SLA-stop bump on `first_response_at` (any non-requester author, monotonic so doesn't re-bump) + ActivityService.record COMMENT entry with `metadata.first_response_bump` flag — emits `tkt.ticket.commented` outside the tx with `firstResponseBumped` flagged in payload; requesters who try `isInternal=true` are silently demoted (the public/internal split is a staff-side concern, surfacing a 400 to a requester whose comment was still saved would confuse). **ProblemService** is admin-only at the service layer for both reads + writes (matches Cycle 6 RefundService precedent) — list / detail with linked-ticket ids inline / create with optional `ticketIds` seeding links / patch with status-RESOLVED-rejected (use /resolve instead) / link with dedupe-against-already-linked / **resolveBatch keystone** locks the problem row + every linked ticket whose status is in (OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/VENDOR_ASSIGNED/PENDING_REQUESTER) using `FOR UPDATE OF t` inside one tenant tx, flips problem to RESOLVED with root_cause+resolution+workaround+resolved_at populated, batch-flips matching tickets to RESOLVED + writes one STATUS_CHANGE activity row per ticket with `metadata.reason='batch resolved via problem ID'` + emits one `tkt.ticket.resolved` per flipped ticket OUTSIDE the tx with `resolvedViaProblemId` + `sourceRefId` populated. 3 Kafka emits total now: `tkt.ticket.submitted`/`assigned`/`resolved` (Step 4) plus `tkt.ticket.commented` (Step 5). Live verified end-to-end on `tenant_demo` (12 scenarios, all pass): activity reads work cross-persona; admin posts public + internal comments correctly bump first_response_at and write activity rows with the bump flag; requester sees public-only filtered list; admin sees all; requester demoted on isInternal=true; Kafka `dev.tkt.ticket.commented` envelope captured with `firstResponseBumped: true`; problem batch-resolve flips T1+T2 from OPEN/IN_PROGRESS to RESOLVED while leaving already-RESOLVED T3 untouched; STATUS_CHANGE activity rows landed with the batch reason; both `dev.tkt.ticket.resolved` envelopes captured with the `resolvedViaProblemId` payload field; 4 permission denials behave correctly (teacher /problems POST 403 + /resolve 403 service-layer admin-only; teacher GET /tickets/:T2/activity 404 row-scope; teacher POST /tickets/:T2/comments 404 same row-scope). Iteration issue caught: `LinkTicketsDto.ticketIds` and `CreateProblemDto.ticketIds` initially shipped without `@IsArray()` + `@IsUUID('all', { each: true })` decorators — the global `ValidationPipe` with `forbidNonWhitelisted: true` silently dropped the property and the smoke saw `"property ticketIds should not exist"` 400; pattern noted for future array DTOs. Smoke residue cleaned (DELETE 3 smoke comments + 3 COMMENT activity rows + 2 STATUS_CHANGE activity rows + revert T1/T2 status + revert problem to INVESTIGATING + drop the smoke link row). Build clean, all 27 ticket routes mapped on boot. **Step 6 ships 2 new Kafka consumers** — `apps/api/src/notifications/consumers/ticket-notification.consumer.ts` (group `ticket-notification-consumer`, subscribed to all 4 `dev.tkt.ticket.*` topics) fans out IN_APP notifications via the Cycle 3 NotificationQueueService; `apps/api/src/tasks/ticket-task-completion.consumer.ts` (group `ticket-task-completion-consumer`, subscribed to `dev.tkt.ticket.resolved`) closes the auto-task DONE-cascade started by the Step 3 seed + Step 4 emit. Both consumers use the standard `unwrapEnvelope` + `processWithIdempotency` claim-after-success pattern. **Notification fan-out matrix:** `tkt.ticket.submitted` → school admins (same `sch-001:admin` IAM cache lookup as AbsenceRequestNotificationConsumer); `tkt.ticket.assigned` → assignee via `payload.recipientAccountId` (Step 4 pre-resolved) with fallback to context-loaded `assigneeAccountId` (vendor assignments deliberately skip this emit per Step 4 design); `tkt.ticket.commented` (public, requester author) → assignee + admins; `tkt.ticket.commented` (public, staff author) → requester only; `tkt.ticket.commented` (internal) → assignee + other admins (NEVER the requester — internal stays staff-side); `tkt.ticket.resolved` → requester only (skipped when requester == resolver). Every payload includes `deep_link: /helpdesk/<ticketId>` so the Step 7 staff UI can navigate from the bell. **Auto-task DONE-cascade flow:** Step 6 consumer runs `UPDATE tsk_tasks SET status='DONE', completed_at=COALESCE(completed_at, now())` against `WHERE source='AUTO' AND source_ref_id=$ticketId AND status NOT IN ('DONE', 'CANCELLED') RETURNING ...` — the WHERE filter is the schema-side belt-and-braces (Kafka redelivery lands a no-op since rows are already DONE). Per flipped row, emits `task.completed` so the Cycle 3 notification pipeline fans out the completion via the existing `task.completed` consumer chain. Plan-time decision documented: **chose a dedicated consumer rather than extending `tsk_auto_task_actions.action_type` with a `MARK_TASK_DONE` value** — the schema's enum is a 3-value CHECK constraint and adding a new value would require a tenant migration + Cycle 7 worker change; a focused consumer is the lighter touch and keeps the TaskWorker centred on creation. Live verified end-to-end on `tenant_demo` (13 scenarios + Redis IN_APP delivery confirmation): teacher submits ticket → `ticket.submitted` lands on admin@ + principal@ inboxes; auto-assignment triggers `ticket.assigned` → principal@ inbox; admin posts public comment → `ticket.commented` → requester (teacher@) inbox with `first_response_bumped=false`; admin posts internal comment → routed to admin@ Platform Admin (Sarah excluded as author), NOT to teacher@; admin resolves → `ticket.resolved` → teacher@ inbox + the auto-task on Sarah's list flips TODO → DONE with `completed_at` populated to the same second; `[ticket-task-completion] flipped 1 auto-task(s) DONE for ticket ...` log line captured. After the 10s NotificationDeliveryWorker tick, both Rivera's and Sarah's `notif:inapp:<accountId>` Redis ZSETs show the matching entries with full payloads — full pipeline (Kafka → consumer → queue → delivery worker → Redis ZADD) verified live. Iteration issue caught (test-data only): smoke SQL used `recipient_account_id` but the actual column is `recipient_id`; fixed and re-ran. Smoke residue cleaned (smoke task + 6 queue rows + smoke ticket CASCADE drops 2 comments + 4 activity rows + Redis ZREMs). Tagged `cycle7-approved` after this commit. **REVIEW-CYCLE7 fixes (2026-05-03):** (BLOCKING 1) `TaskWorker` now rethrows on ack/task INSERT failure and releases the Redis dedup claim before rethrowing, so `processWithIdempotency` does NOT claim the Kafka event*id and redelivery retries the failed owner — owners whose INSERT already succeeded keep their Redis claim and skip cleanly on retry. Acknowledgement inserts gain matching Redis SET NX dedup at `tsk:auto-ack:{subdomain}:{ownerId}:{sourceRefId}` plus a new `lookupExistingAck(ownerId, sourceRefId, sourceTable)` helper so a redelivered event whose ack already landed can link the existing ack id into the companion CREATE_TASK action. (BLOCKING 2) `POST /approvals` re-gated from `ops-001:write` (held by Teacher / Parent / Student / Staff per Step 3 seed) to `ops-001:admin` (admin-only). Domain modules (LeaveService, future ChildLinkRequestService) submit programmatically via `WorkflowEngineService.submit()` and bypass the gate. Verified live: teacher / parent POST /approvals → 403; admin with disallowed table → 400 allowlist; admin with bogus row id → 400 not found.
