**Step 3 lands `013_hr_certifications_and_training.sql`** with 5 base tables: `hr_staff_certifications` (10 certification types — TEACHING_LICENCE, FIRST_AID, SAFEGUARDING_LEVEL1/2, DBS_BASIC/ENHANCED, FOOD_HYGIENE, FIRE_SAFETY_WARDEN, SPECIALIST_SUBJECT, CUSTOM; 4 verification statuses — PENDING/VERIFIED/EXPIRED/REVOKED; ADR-015 followed — `reference_number` + `verification_status` only, never raw DBS payload), `hr_training_requirements` (school-scoped with optional `position_id` for position-specific mandates, frequency CHECK ONE_TIME/ANNUAL/BIENNIAL/TRIENNIAL/CUSTOM with a multi-column CHECK that `custom_frequency_months` is set if and only if frequency=CUSTOM), `hr_training_compliance` (UNIQUE(employee_id, requirement_id) materialised state with `linked_certification_id` FK to `hr_staff_certifications ON DELETE SET NULL` so a deleted cert leaves the compliance row defensible), `hr_cpd_requirements` (UNIQUE(school_id, position_id, academic_year_id) PD/credit-hour mandates), `hr_work_authorisation` (UNIQUE(employee_id) right-to-work with 6 document types, separate `expiry_date` and `reverification_due_date` because some EADs require re-verification on a different cadence). 7 intra-tenant FKs (4 to `hr_employees ON DELETE CASCADE`, 1 to `hr_training_requirements ON DELETE CASCADE`, 1 to `hr_staff_certifications ON DELETE SET NULL`, 1 each to `hr_positions` and `sis_academic_years` non-cascade). Tenant logical base table count: 71 (was 66). Live verification on `tenant_demo`: 7 CHECKs fire (cert type/status/dates, requirement frequency/custom, work-auth type, CPD pd_hours), happy path inserts succeed (Rivera Teaching Licence VERIFIED, ANNUAL renewal requirement, compliance row linked to the cert), ON DELETE SET NULL nulls `linked_certification_id` when the cert is deleted while keeping the compliance row, UNIQUE on `(employee_id, requirement_id)` and on `hr_work_authorisation.employee_id` rejects duplicates, ON DELETE CASCADE on `hr_employees` drops cert + compliance + work_auth rows for a temp employee.

**Step 2 lands `012_hr_leave_management.sql`** with 3 base tables: `hr_leave_types` (per-school catalogue with `is_paid` / `accrual_rate` / optional `max_balance`), `hr_leave_balances` (UNIQUE(employee_id, leave_type_id, academic_year_id) with `accrued`/`used`/`pending NUMERIC(5,2)` non-negative CHECKs, FK to `sis_academic_years`), and `hr_leave_requests` (status CHECK PENDING/APPROVED/REJECTED/CANCELLED, half-day `days_requested NUMERIC(4,1) > 0` CHECK, dates CHECK `end_date >= start_date`, an `is_hr_initiated` boolean with a multi-column CHECK that enforces `(is_hr_initiated, hr_initiated_by, hr_initiated_reason)` are all-set or all-null together for HR-initiated leaves like mandatory PD days). 5 intra-tenant FKs (2 to `hr_employees ON DELETE CASCADE`, 2 to `hr_leave_types`, 1 to `sis_academic_years` non-cascade). Tenant logical base table count: 66 (was 63). Approval flow is documented at the schema layer; the Step 7 LeaveService will own the state transitions and Kafka emits (`hr.leave.requested`, `hr.leave.approved`, `hr.leave.rejected`, `hr.leave.cancelled`). Live verification on `tenant_demo`: all 6 CHECKs fire (status, dates, days, hr-initiated mismatch, accrual_rate negative, balance accrued negative), UNIQUE on `(employee, type, year)` rejects duplicates, happy-path multi-insert across all 3 tables succeeds, ON DELETE CASCADE drops 1 balance + 1 request when a temp `hr_employees` row is deleted.

**Step 1 lands 5 base tables on top of `hr_employees`** in the same `011_hr_employees_and_positions.sql` migration: `hr_positions` (UNIQUE(school_id, title), soft FK `department_id` → `sis_departments`, `is_teaching_role` flag), `hr_employee_positions` (DB-enforced FKs to `hr_employees ON DELETE CASCADE` and `hr_positions`, `effective_from`/`effective_to` date range, partial UNIQUE index on `(employee_id) WHERE is_primary AND effective_to IS NULL` so an employee has at most one current primary position, FTE CHECK 0 < fte ≤ 1.000), `hr_emergency_contacts` (CASCADE on employee delete, partial UNIQUE on `is_primary`), `hr_document_types` (UNIQUE(school_id, name), retention_days CHECK > 0), `hr_employee_documents` (CASCADE on employee delete, FK to document_type, signed-URL pattern documented in COMMENT). 5 new intra-tenant FKs, 0 cross-schema FKs. Tenant logical base table count: 63 (was 58). Live verification on `tenant_demo`: every CHECK fires (dates, FTE > 1.0, retention 0, file_size_bytes -1), FK rejection returns clean ERROR, happy-path multi-insert across all 5 tables succeeds, ON DELETE CASCADE on a temporary `hr_employees` row drops all 3 child rows. **Splitter trap struck again** — even block-comment text containing `;` cuts the migration mid-comment because the splitter splits on every semicolon regardless of quoting context (block-comment, string literal, anywhere). The 011 migration's header now spells out "no semicolons" instead of using a literal `;` as an example.

`packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/011_hr_employees_and_positions.sql` lands the `hr_employees` table (soft FK to `platform.iam_person` via UNIQUE `person_id` + soft FK to `platform.platform_users` via UNIQUE `account_id`, CHECK constraints on `employment_type` + `employment_status`). `packages/database/src/seed-hr.ts` (idempotent, gated on `hr_employees` row count) inserts 4 employee rows for `tenant_demo` (Mitchell as `principal@`, Rivera as `teacher@`, Park as `vp@`, Hayes as `counsellor@`; the `admin@` Platform Admin persona is intentionally NOT bridged), then runs four `UPDATE … FROM hr_employees WHERE e.person_id = t.column` bridge statements inside one `executeInTenantTransaction` to re-point `sis_class_teachers.teacher_employee_id`, `cls_grades.teacher_id`, `cls_lessons.teacher_id`, and `cls_student_progress_notes.author_id` from `iam_person.id` to `hr_employees.id`. Live verification: 4 rows inserted, 6 + 62 + 0 + 1 rows bridged across the four columns, 0 orphans. `ActorContextService.resolveActor(...)` now populates `actor.employeeId: string | null` via a tenant-scoped lookup against `hr_employees.person_id`. All staff-side service code substituted: `attendance.service.ts::assertCanWriteClassAttendance`, `assignment.service.ts::{assertCanWriteClass,canReadClass STAFF,isClassManager}`, `gradebook.service.ts::{assertCanViewStudent STAFF,isClassManager}`, `submission.service.ts::{isManagerOfClass,canSeeSubmission STAFF}`, `grade.service.ts::{batchGrade,upsertGrade}` (4 INSERT/UPDATE sites; `ForbiddenException` guard at entry of both write paths since `cls_grades.teacher_id` is NOT NULL), `progress-note.service.ts::{upsert,listForStudent STAFF,assertCanViewStudent STAFF}` (2 INSERT/UPDATE sites + `ForbiddenException` guard), `student.service.ts::visibilityClause STAFF`, `class.service.ts::listForTeacherEmployee` (renamed from `listForTeacherPerson`), `class.service.ts::loadTeachersForClasses` (now joins through `hr_employees` to `iam_person`), `class.controller.ts::my` (resolves actor + passes `actor.employeeId`), `audience-fan-out.worker.ts::audienceClass` (joins through `hr_employees.account_id` to `platform_users` for the CLASS-audience teacher branch). `IamModule` now imports `TenantModule` so `ActorContextService` can do the tenant-scoped lookup. Live smoke against `tenant_demo` confirmed `GET /classes/my` for `teacher@` returns the same 6 classes as before the bridge, `GET /students` returns 15 students via the new STAFF predicate, `GET /classes/:id/gradebook` returns 3 students for the P1 class. The four `COMMENT ON COLUMN` annotations on the bridged columns were rewritten to retire the "TEMPORARY HR-EMPLOYEE IDENTITY MAPPING" wording and adopt the permanent "Soft FK to hr_employees(id) per ADR-055" convention. **Migration gotcha re-confirmed:** the SQL splitter in `provision-tenant.ts` cuts on `;` even inside string literals — the `COMMENT ON COLUMN` strings cannot contain `;`. Use commas / "and" / em-dashes instead. Tripped this on the first re-provision attempt; the four updated COMMENTs and the new `hr_employees.school_id` COMMENT all use punctuation that survives the splitter. (Steps 1–10 of Cycle 4 — schemas for positions, leave, certifications, training compliance, onboarding, and the corresponding NestJS modules + UI + CAT — remain to ship; see `docs/campusos-cycle4-implementation-plan.html` and `HANDOFF-CYCLE4.md` for the plan.)

Cycle 0 (Platform Foundation) is COMPLETE. **Cycle 1 (SIS Core + Attendance) is COMPLETE — all 11 steps done. Post-cycle architecture review fixes (REVIEW-CYCLE1) landed.** Schema, seed, SIS API, Attendance API + Kafka emits, web UI shell, Teacher Dashboard, Attendance Taking UI with confirm modal + batch submit, Parent Dashboard + child attendance calendar + absence-request form, AdminDashboard (school-wide overview + pending-absence queue), and a verified end-to-end vertical slice (`docs/cycle1-cat-script.md`). The post-cycle review tightened tenant isolation (`SET LOCAL search_path` inside an interactive tx), added row-level authorization to student reads, gated attendance writes by `sis_class_teachers` membership, and replaced cross-scope admin checks with a tenant-scope-chain check.

**Cycle 3 (Communications) is COMPLETE — all 11 steps done. Post-cycle architecture review APPROVED at commit `592d366` (REVIEW-CYCLE3-CHATGPT — final verdict).** Round 1 at SHA `7c7e3d4` returned REJECT pending two reliability/security fixes; Round 2 at SHA `592d366` returned APPROVED to proceed. The three findings (BLOCKING 1 Kafka consumer error swallowing, BLOCKING 2 NotificationDeliveryWorker SENT-as-in-flight, MAJOR overly-broad `isManager()` scope) were all fixed in `592d366`.

**Reviewer's carry-over for the next cycle:** verify `platform.platform_dlq_messages` is in migrations + available in every environment since the consumer now depends on it. **Already satisfied at the schema level** — the table ships in the platform Prisma migration `20260427211003_add_communications_platform_tables` (Cycle 3 Step 3), and the CI workflow runs `npx prisma migrate deploy --schema=prisma/platform/schema.prisma` so the table is created in CI + every environment that follows the deploy pipeline. The remaining work is operational: wire a DLQ-row dashboard / alert into Phase 2 so a parked poison message gets human attention, not just a quiet row in a table. **REVIEW-CYCLE3 fixes (2026-04-28):** (1) `apps/api/src/kafka/kafka-consumer.service.ts` no longer swallows handler failures — it rethrows so KafkaJS retains the offset, with an in-memory `(group, topic, partition, offset) → attempts` map bounded by `MAX_HANDLER_ATTEMPTS=5` so a poison message gets parked into `platform.platform_dlq_messages` (with `event_id`, `tenant_id`, headers, payload, error_class/message, retry_count) and committed instead of looping forever. Malformed JSON is parked directly without retry since it can't recover. (2) `apps/api/src/notifications/notification-delivery.worker.ts` now uses a proper `PROCESSING` in-flight state — Phase 0 stale-row sweep recovers PROCESSING > 5 minutes back to PENDING (preserving attempts so MAX_ATTEMPTS still bounds total retries), Phase 1 SELECT FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED + UPDATE PROCESSING + processing_started_at, Phase 2 deliver outside the claim tx then UPDATE SENT (only after Redis ZADD + `msg_notification_log` write succeed). New tenant migration `010_msg_queue_processing_state.sql` extends the queue status CHECK to include PROCESSING and adds a `processing_started_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column + partial index. The migration uses `/* */` block-comment style — line `--` headers cause the SQL splitter to filter the entire first statement. Tenant base table count: still 57 (only constraint + column added). (3) `apps/api/src/announcements/announcement.service.ts::isManager()` no longer returns true for any actor with `personType === 'STAFF'` — it now calls `permissionCheckService.hasAnyPermissionInTenant(accountId, schoolId, ['com-002:write'])` so manager status follows the actual permission grant. The IAM seed gives Staff just `com-002:read`, so a staff member is correctly treated as a reader (audience-row-required, no draft visibility) instead of a manager. School admins still bypass via `actor.isSchoolAdmin`. All 4 call sites in the service await the new async helper. CAT smoke (all 7 scenarios) re-verified live against `tenant_demo` after the fixes; queue inspection post-run shows zero rows stuck in PROCESSING (80/80 SENT). Step 1 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/007_msg_messaging.sql` — 6 base messaging tables (`msg_thread_types`, `msg_threads`, `msg_thread_participants`, `msg_messages`, `msg_message_attachments`, `msg_message_reads`) plus 64 HASH partitions of `msg_threads` (per ADR-047, partition key `school_id`) and 24 monthly RANGE partitions of `msg_messages` covering 2025-08 → 2027-08. Soft UUID refs to `platform.platform_users` per ADR-001/020. Composite PKs `(id, school_id)` for `msg_threads` and `(id, created_at)` for `msg_messages` (the partition columns must appear in the unique constraint). FKs from child tables to the partitioned parents follow the `sis_attendance_evidence` precedent — denormalise the partition key onto the child + add an index, no DB-enforced FK. **Step 2 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/008_msg_notifications_and_announcements.sql` — 7 base tables (`msg_alert_types`, `msg_notification_queue`, `msg_notification_preferences`, `msg_notification_log`, `msg_announcements`, `msg_announcement_audiences`, `msg_announcement_reads`) plus 24 monthly RANGE partitions of `msg_notification_log` (sent_at, 2025-08 → 2027-08, matching the messages window). `msg_notification_log` uses composite PK `(id, sent_at)` so the partition column is in the unique constraint. The notification queue does NOT carry a UNIQUE constraint on `idempotency_key` — the partial index on it is for read-side dedup investigation only; authoritative idempotency is Redis SET NX (per the plan, to avoid deadlocks during emergency fan-out). 3 intra-tenant DB-enforced FKs (`msg_announcements.alert_type_id → msg_alert_types`, `msg_announcement_audiences.announcement_id → msg_announcements ON DELETE CASCADE`, `msg_announcement_reads.announcement_id → msg_announcements ON DELETE CASCADE`); all other cross-table refs (queue→user, log→queue, audiences→user) are soft per ADR-001/020. `msg_announcements.audience_ref TEXT` is a polymorphic target field interpreted by the AudienceFanOutWorker (Step 7) based on `audience_type` (CLASS = sis_classes.id as text, YEAR_GROUP = grade label, ROLE = iam role name, ALL_SCHOOL = NULL).** **Step 3 lands `packages/database/prisma/tenant/migrations/009_msg_moderation.sql` (6 base tenant tables — `msg_moderation_policies`, `msg_moderation_log`, `msg_tags`, `msg_user_tags`, `msg_user_blocks`, `msg_admin_access_log` — plus 24 monthly RANGE partitions of `msg_moderation_log` matching the 2025-08 → 2027-08 window) AND a Prisma migration `20260427211003_add_communications_platform_tables` that adds the first platform-schema tables since Cycle 0: `platform_push_tokens` (per-(user, device) push registration with `PushPlatform` enum IOS/ANDROID/WEB) and `platform_dlq_messages` (Kafka dead letter queue with topic/partition/kafka_offset/payload/headers/retry_count/error fields). The moderation policy table is three-tier (PLATFORM/DISTRICT/BUILDING) with each tenant carrying its own copy of the higher-tier policies that apply to it, so the moderation interceptor consults a single tenant table without cross-schema reads. The moderation log holds a soft denormalised `(message_id, message_created_at)` ref to the partitioned `msg_messages` (matches the `msg_message_attachments` precedent from Step 1) plus a DB-enforced `policy_id → msg_moderation_policies(id)` FK that PostgreSQL replicates onto each of the 24 monthly partitions (25 FK rows in `pg_constraint`, expected). `msg_user_tags.tag_id → msg_tags(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` is the second DB-enforced FK; `msg_user_blocks` carries a `blocker_id <> blocked_id` self-block CHECK; `msg_admin_access_log.reason` is NOT NULL because every admin read of a non-participant private thread must be FERPA-justified.** **Step 4 lands `packages/database/src/seed-messaging.ts` (idempotent, gated on `msg_thread_types` row count) — populates the demo tenant with 4 thread types (TEACHER_PARENT, CLASS_DISCUSSION, ADMIN_STAFF, SYSTEM_NOTIFICATION), 3 sample threads + 6 participants + 10 messages + 4 message reads (Rivera↔David Chen Maya progress check-in, P1 Algebra Q&A class discussion, Sarah↔Rivera staff thread), 3 alert types (GENERAL_ANNOUNCEMENT, PARENT_INFORMATIONAL, WEATHER_CLOSURE), 40 notification preferences (5 test users × 8 notification types — `attendance.{tardy,absent}`, `grade.published`, `progress_note.published`, `absence.{requested,reviewed}`, `message.posted`, `announcement.published` — with EMAIL channel enabled for `grade.published` + `attendance.tardy`, and 22:00–07:00 quiet hours on the parent), 2 moderation policies (PLATFORM-scope BLOCK profanity policy + BUILDING-scope FLAG_FOR_REVIEW policy for substance/bullying keywords), 2 announcements (ALL_SCHOOL "Welcome Back" + ROLE=PARENT "Parent-Teacher Conference Dates", both published) with 15 audience rows + 4 read marks, and 3 notification queue rows (1 SENT tardy notification for David, 1 SENT grade.published for Maya, 1 PENDING message-posted for David) plus 2 corresponding notification_log delivery rows. Adds `seed:messaging` to `packages/database/package.json`. The seed targets `tenant_demo` only (matching `seed-sis` / `seed-classroom` precedent — `tenant_test` stays empty for tests). `seed-iam.ts` was extended to add `COM-002:read` to the Student and Staff roles (previously held only by Teacher/Parent/School Admin/Platform Admin) — closes the plan's "Announcements read to all + write to Teacher/Admin" requirement; COM-001 read/write was already on Teacher/Parent/Student, COM-003 already on Platform/School Admin via `everyFunction`. Effective access cache rebuilt: Student now 15 perms (was 14), Staff now 6 (was 5).** **Step 5 lands the notification pipeline at `apps/api/src/notifications/`: 5 Kafka consumers (AttendanceNotificationConsumer for `att.student.marked_tardy` + `att.student.marked_absent`, GradeNotificationConsumer for `cls.grade.published`, ProgressNoteNotificationConsumer for `cls.progress_note.published`, AbsenceRequestNotificationConsumer for `att.absence.requested` + `att.absence.reviewed`, and the forward-compatible MessageNotificationConsumer for `msg.message.posted` whose producer ships in Step 6) + `NotificationQueueService` + `NotificationDeliveryWorker` + `RedisService` (first ioredis usage in the API). Each consumer follows the Cycle 2 GradebookSnapshotWorker pattern: read the ADR-057 envelope (with header fallback), `IdempotencyService.isClaimed` read-only check on arrival, resolve recipients inside `runWithTenantContextAsync`, enqueue via `NotificationQueueService`, then `claim()` after success (REVIEW-CYCLE2 BLOCKING 2 at-least-once). Recipient resolution: attendance/grade/progress-note consumers fan out to portal-enabled guardians via `sis_student_guardians` (`portal_access=true AND receives_reports=true AND g.account_id IS NOT NULL`); grade + progress-note also notify the student themself via the `sis_students → platform_students → platform_users` lookup; absence-request fans out to school admins (resolved via the `sch-001:admin` row in `iam_effective_access_cache` joined to `iam_scope`/`iam_scope_type`, school + platform scopes per ADR-036) on submission and to the original submitter (`sis_absence_requests.submitted_by`) on review; message-posted notifies every active non-sender thread participant + bumps the per-(user, thread) Redis HASH unread counter that Step 6's UnreadCountService reads. `NotificationQueueService.enqueue()` does Redis SET NX on `notif:idem:{subdomain}:{key}` (7-day TTL — authoritative dedup per ADR-001/020 since the DB table has no UNIQUE on `idempotency_key`), reads `msg_notification_preferences` for the (recipient, type) pair (default IN_APP if missing), checks 22:00–07:00-style quiet hours (wraps midnight; defers `scheduled_for` to the next quiet-end boundary), and INSERTs a PENDING row with the supplied JSON payload + `correlation_id`. `NotificationDeliveryWorker` polls every 10s (configurable via `NOTIFICATION_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`) — for every active row in `platform.schools` it enters tenant context, runs `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 25` on `msg_notification_queue` for `status='PENDING' AND scheduled_for <= now()`, flips rows to SENT in the same tx (atomic in-flight flag), then per-row delivers IN_APP via Redis ZADD on `notif:inapp:{accountId}` (capped at 100 entries via ZREMRANGEBYRANK) and stubs EMAIL/PUSH/SMS with a `[stub-deliver]` log line. Every delivery (real or stub) writes a `msg_notification_log` row with `status=DELIVERED` for IN_APP and `SENT` for the stubbed channels. Failure path: bumps `attempts`, flips back to PENDING with a 30-second `scheduled_for` backoff for up to MAX_ATTEMPTS=5 retries before settling on FAILED. Best-effort connection: RedisService and KafkaConsumerService both no-op gracefully when Redis or Kafka are unreachable in dev (matching the existing KafkaProducerService pattern). NotificationsModule wired into AppModule between ClassroomModule and the global guards. Build verified clean (`pnpm --filter @campusos/api build`); live boot confirmed all 5 consumer subscriptions + worker poll registration + Redis connect on 2026-04-27.** **Step 6 lands the messaging request-path module at `apps/api/src/messaging/`: `ThreadService` (create / list / read / archive — validates participants against `msg_thread_types.allowed_participant_roles` by mapping each account's IAM role names through `iam_role_assignment + iam_role + iam_scope` to the synthetic role tokens used by the seed; auto-adds the creator as OWNER; refuses pairs that have a `msg_user_blocks` row in either direction; writes a `msg_admin_access_log` row whenever a school admin reads a thread they aren't a participant in — FERPA audit), `MessageService` (post / edit / soft-delete / list — every post runs through `ContentModerationService.evaluate()` BEFORE insert; BLOCKED → HTTP 422 with the generic policy message + `msg_moderation_log` row + no `msg_messages` row; FLAGGED / ESCALATED → message persists with the matching `moderation_status` + `msg_moderation_log` row; CLEAN → status='CLEAN'; on success bumps `msg_threads.last_message_at`, auto-inserts a self-read receipt for the sender so they don't see a badge for their own message, increments the per-other-participant Redis HASH `inbox:{accountId}` synchronously, then emits `msg.message.posted` so the Step 5 MessageNotificationConsumer fans out IN*APP notifications + a second HASH bump (idempotent — same key)), `UnreadCountService` (Redis-backed: increment / clearThread / getBadgeCount / getByThread — never queries `msg_messages`), `ContentModerationService` (loads every active `msg_moderation_policies` row in one tenant-scoped query; whole-word case-insensitive keyword matching with regex escape — Scunthorpe-safe; "most restrictive wins" = BLOCKED > ESCALATED > FLAGGED > CLEAN; on a non-CLEAN verdict writes a `msg_moderation_log` row with `flag_type` + `severity` + `matched_keywords` + `review_outcome='PENDING'`; degrades gracefully to CLEAN on lookup failure so a transient platform DB blip can't shut messaging down). 10 endpoints under `com-001:read` / `com-001:write`: `GET /threads`, `GET /threads/:id`, `POST /threads` (with optional `initialMessage`), `POST /threads/:id/read` (idempotent — inserts `msg_message_reads` rows + bumps `last_read_at` + clears the per-thread Redis HASH entry), `PATCH /threads/:id/archive`, `GET /threads/:threadId/messages` (newest-first keyset pagination via `before` cursor), `POST /threads/:threadId/messages`, `PATCH /messages/:id` (author-only, 15-minute window — re-runs moderation), `DELETE /messages/:id` (sender or school admin; idempotent soft-delete; the `body` is blanked in `rowToDto` so the timeline reads "Message deleted" without breaking flow), `GET /notifications/unread-count` (top-bar badge — sums Redis HASH; `com-001:read OR com-002:read` so the bell renders for every persona that holds either communications read tier). `RedisService` was extended with `clearUnread`, `sumUnread`, and `listUnreadByThread`. `ThreadService.create()` validates that every account holds at least one of the thread type's `allowed_participant_roles` via the `iam_role_assignment + iam_role + iam_scope_type` join filtered to the school + platform scopes per ADR-036; the role-name token mapping is `name.toUpperCase().replace(/\s+/g, '*')`so`"School Admin"`→`"SCHOOL_ADMIN"`matches the seed shape. Build verified clean; live smoke against`tenant_demo`on 2026-04-27 confirmed: clean post returns 201 with`senderName="James Rivera"`(joined through`platform.platform_users → iam_person`); BLOCK keyword `shit`returns 422 with "This message was not sent because it contains content that violates school policy." and lands a`BLOCKED / URGENT / {shit}`row in`msg_moderation_log`; FLAG keyword `weed`returns 201 with`moderationStatus="FLAGGED"`plus a`FLAGGED / WARNING / {weed}`log row; multi-word BLOCK phrase`kill you`triggers 422 +`BLOCKED / URGENT / {"kill you"}`; parent's `GET /threads`shows`unread=6`for the TEACHER_PARENT thread;`POST /threads/:id/read`returns`marked=4`; `GET /notifications/unread-count`drops to`total=0` immediately after.** **Step 7 lands the announcements module at `apps/api/src/announcements/`: `AnnouncementService` (list / get / create / patch-draft / mark-read / stats — manager scope = School Admin OR `personType=STAFF`, reader scope = audience-row-required; published one-way once flipped from draft so the audience fan-out is stable), `AudienceFanOutWorker` (Kafka consumer on `msg.announcement.published` under group `audience-fan-out-worker`; resolves audience by `audience_type` and writes one row per recipient into `msg_announcement_audiences` with `delivery_status='DELIVERED'` ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, then enqueues an `announcement.published` notification per recipient via the Step 5 `NotificationQueueService`), and a 6-endpoint `AnnouncementController` under `com-002:read` / `com-002:write`: `GET /announcements`, `GET /announcements/:id`, `POST /announcements` (draft or publish-now), `PATCH /announcements/:id` (edit a draft; flipping `isPublished=true` publishes + emits), `POST /announcements/:id/read` (idempotent — inserts `msg_announcement_reads` row, also flips the matching `msg_announcement_audiences` row PENDING→DELIVERED), `GET /announcements/:id/stats` (author-or-admin only — total audience, read count, read %, delivery breakdown). Audience resolution per `audience_type`: ALL*SCHOOL = every account with any active `iam_role_assignment` in the school+platform scope chain; CLASS = active enrollees + their portal-enabled guardians + assigned teachers via `sis_class_teachers` (with the Cycle 2 `teacher_employee_id = iam_person.id` mapping); YEAR_GROUP = students with `sis_students.grade_level = audience_ref` + their portal-enabled guardians; ROLE = accounts whose IAM role token (`UPPER(REGEXP_REPLACE(name, '\s+', '*', 'g'))`) equals `audience_ref`; CUSTOM is reserved for the deferred Communication Groups feature and logs+drops. The worker reuses the Step 5 `unwrapEnvelope`+`processWithIdempotency`helpers (claim-after-success, REVIEW-CYCLE2 BLOCKING 2). The publish path is one-way at the request layer: editing an already-published announcement returns 400, and PATCH cannot un-publish. The first kafka topic auto-creation race that affects gradebook-snapshot-worker on a fresh dev cluster also affects the new worker; pre-creating`dev.msg.announcement.published`once with`kafka-topics.sh --create` is the dev workaround.** **Step 11 lands the Cycle 3 CAT at `docs/cycle3-cat-script.md` — a 7-scenario reproducible end-to-end walkthrough verified live against `tenant_demo`: tardy mark → AttendanceNotificationConsumer → queue → NotificationDeliveryWorker → parent's bell (`unreadCount=15` with `attendance.tardy student=Maya Chen status=TARDY` at the top); grade publish → GradeNotificationConsumer → fan-out to student + portal-enabled guardians (parent gets `Quadratics Homework Set score=88`); direct messaging compose + reply + mark-read across personas; BLOCK keyword `shit` → 422 with the exact UI policy string + `BLOCKED / URGENT / {shit}` row in `msg_moderation_log`; ALL_SCHOOL announcement publish → AudienceFanOutWorker resolves 5 recipients → mark-read flips `readCount=1 readPercentage=20`; `is_enabled=false` pref disables enqueue (BEFORE=3 AFTER=3 unchanged); 3 permission denials (403 com-002:write, 403 method-tier on stats, 404 row-scope on non-participant thread). The script also documents and ships a fix for a latent Step 5 bug caught by this run — `GradeNotificationConsumer.loadContext`, `AttendanceNotificationConsumer.loadContext`, and `ProgressNoteNotificationConsumer.loadContext` all referenced a `c.title` column on `sis_classes` that doesn't exist (the actual class display name comes from `sis_courses.name` joined through `course_id`); all three SQL queries fixed to JOIN `sis_courses co ON co.id = c.course_id` and read `co.name || ' (' || c.section_code || ')'`. The bug never tripped before because Steps 5/6/7/8's smoke tests didn't exercise an end-to-end Kafka event through one of these three consumers — Step 5 verified consumer subscriptions, Steps 6/7 used different consumers, and Step 8's bell drove from PRE-EXISTING seeded queue rows. Documents David Chen's seeded 22:00–07:00 UTC quiet hours as the reason the CAT clears + restores them for Scenarios 1–5 (Scenario 6 covers preference honoring via `is_enabled=false` instead, which is the cleaner assertion).** **Step 10 lands the announcements UI: 3 routes under `/announcements` plus a hooks file. Backend was already complete in Step 7 (6 endpoints under `com-002:read` / `com-002:write`); Step 10 is purely additive on the web side. `apps/web/src/lib/types.ts` adds `AudienceType` (`'ALL_SCHOOL' | 'CLASS' | 'YEAR_GROUP' | 'ROLE' | 'CUSTOM'`), `AnnouncementDto`, `AnnouncementStatsDto`, `CreateAnnouncementPayload`, `UpdateAnnouncementPayload`, `MarkAnnouncementReadResponse`. `apps/web/src/hooks/use-announcements.ts` (new file) exposes 6 hooks: `useAnnouncements({includeDrafts, includeExpired})` (30s poll, refetch-on-focus), `useAnnouncement(id)` (one-shot), `useAnnouncementStats(id)` (30s poll, only enabled when `id` is non-null — the detail page only enables this for authors/admins so non-managers don't 403 the worker), `useCreateAnnouncement`, `useUpdateAnnouncement(id)`, `useMarkAnnouncementRead`. Uses the existing Toast for success/error feedback. Body rendered in `whitespace-pre-wrap`. Auto-marks-read on first mount when published-and-unread, tracked via `lastMarkedReadFor` ref so the cache invalidation poll doesn't re-fire it. Stats panel below the article renders for authors and school admins only — total-audience / read-count + % / delivered / pending+failed metrics plus a campus-600 progress bar. Both reuse the same `iam_role_assignment + roles + iam_scope + iam_scope_type` join shape as the existing `loadRoleTokens` (school + platform scope chain per ADR-036). **Latent bug found and fixed during smoke**: both `loadRoleTokens` and the new `listRecipients` referenced `platform.iam_role` (doesn't exist — actual table is `platform.roles`); both queries now point at the right relation. The dormant bug never tripped before because Step 6 only invoked `loadRoleTokens` from `ThreadService.create()`, and the seeded threads were created via the seed script (which writes directly), not via the API. archive toggle flips `isArchived=true` cleanly.** **Step 8 lands the NotificationBell + dropdown + `/notifications` page: extends `RedisService` with `listInAppNotifications` (ZREVRANGE on `notif:inapp:{accountId}`), `countInAppSince` (ZCOUNT exclusive of lastReadAt), and `getNotificationLastReadAt` / `setNotificationLastReadAt` (`notif:lastread:{accountId}` epoch-ms key, no TTL — bounded by active-user count); adds `NotificationInboxService` (read side) + `NotificationInboxController` with three endpoints under `com-001:read OR com-002:read`: `GET /notifications/inbox?limit=10` (returns `{ unreadCount, items, lastReadAt }` for the bell — items come from the Redis sorted set populated by `NotificationDeliveryWorker`, score=epoch ms, items have `isRead=score<=lastReadAt`), `GET /notifications/history?limit=25&type=…&before=…` (paginated history straight from `msg_notification_queue`, keyset cursor on `COALESCE(sent_at, created_at)`, server-side type filter is exact-match on `notification_type`), and `POST /notifications/mark-all-read` (bumps `notif:lastread:{accountId}` to `Date.now()` — idempotent, single-call clear; per-item dismissal not in scope this phase). The legacy `GET /notifications/unread-count` (Step 6) stays put — it returns the per-thread messaging unread map for the messaging UI's inbox-row badges; the bell uses `/inbox` for the unified count instead. Web side: `useNotificationInbox` polls every 30s + refetches on focus; `useNotificationHistory` is a one-shot read; `useMarkAllNotificationsRead` invalidates `['notifications']`. `NotificationBell` mounts in `TopBar` for any user with `com-001:read OR com-002:read` (every seeded persona qualifies), renders the badge (capped at "99+") and a 22rem dropdown of the last 10 items; each item runs through `describeNotification(item)` (per-type title + subtitle from the consumer-shaped payload — message_posted reads `sender_name` + `thread_subject` + `preview`, grade.published reads `assignment_title` + `student_name` + `percentage`, attendance.tardy reads `student_name` + `class_name` + `period`, etc.) and `resolveDeepLink(item, user)` (persona-aware for grade.published — STUDENT → `deep_link_student`, GUARDIAN → `deep_link_guardian`; falls back to `payload.deep_link` for the other types, with a per-type fallback for legacy-shaped payloads that lack the field — e.g. message.posted falls back to `/messages/{thread_id}`). Type-specific icons (`AttendanceIcon`, `GradeIcon`, `ChatBubbleIcon`, `MegaphoneIcon`, `CheckCircleIcon`, `BellIcon`) and tone-mapped pill colors are added to `apps/web/src/components/shell/icons.tsx`. The `/notifications` page lives at `apps/web/src/app/(app)/notifications/page.tsx` — full paginated list with 6 client-side filter chips (All / Attendance + Absence / Grades / Progress notes / Messages / Announcements — grouped chips that don't map 1:1 to a single notification_type filter on the backend), `Mark all read` action in the page header, unread items highlighted with a blue dot + tinted row background. Build verified clean (`pnpm --filter @campusos/api build` + `pnpm --filter @campusos/web build` — `/notifications` ships as a static route at 1.98 kB First Load JS); live smoke against `tenant_demo` on 2026-04-27 confirmed parent (David Chen) sees 1 unread on first load (the seeded PENDING `message.posted` queue row, delivered to Redis by the worker), `mark-all-read` returns `{ lastReadAt: 1777331319341 }` and the next `/inbox` poll returns `unreadCount=0` with `isRead=true`; type filter `?type=message.posted` returns 4 items all of that type; type filter `?type=announcement.published` returns 4; cursor pagination with `before=...` returns subsequent pages with newer-first ordering preserved.** Tenant base table count: 57 (was 51). Provisioned cleanly + idempotently to demo + test on 2026-04-27. CHECK constraints (`role`, `moderation_status`, `severity`, queue `status`, log `channel`, log `status`, `audience_type`, audience `delivery_status`, mod-policy `scope`, mod-policy `keyword_action`, sensitivity_threshold `0–100`, mod-log `flag_type`, mod-log `severity`, mod-log `review_outcome`, user-block self-block) verified live; 0 cross-schema FKs. `apps/api/src/kafka/event-envelope.ts` defines the `EventEnvelope` interface (`event_id`, `event_type`, `event_version`, `occurred_at`, `published_at`, `tenant_id`, `source_module`, `correlation_id`, `payload`) plus the `prefixedTopic(name)` helper that applies the `KAFKA_TOPIC_ENV` prefix (default `dev`) to every topic on the wire. `KafkaProducerService.emit()` was rewritten to take an `EmitOptions` object — wraps the domain payload in the envelope, env-prefixes the topic, and continues to set the three legacy transport headers (`event-id`, `tenant-id`, `tenant-subdomain`) plus a new `event-type` header for backward compatibility. All 12 existing emit sites migrated (`grade.service.ts` × 2, `submission.service.ts` × 1, `progress-note.service.ts` × 1, `attendance.service.ts` × 6, `absence-request.service.ts` × 2). `GradebookSnapshotWorker.handle()` now reads `event_id` / `tenant_id` from the envelope first, falls back to transport headers for any in-flight legacy messages; subscribes to `prefixedTopic('cls.grade.published')` / `…unpublished`. Every Cycle 3 consumer that lands in Steps 5+ will read directly from the envelope. See `HANDOFF-CYCLE3.md` for the Step 0 verification record and Steps 1–11 plan.

**Cycle 2 (Classroom + Assignments + Grading) is COMPLETE — all 10 steps done. Post-cycle architecture review APPROVED at commit `d876e86` (REVIEW-CYCLE2-CHATGPT — final verdict).** The post-cycle review tightened `GET /classes/:classId/gradebook` to manager-only (permission gate switched from `tch-003:read` → `tch-003:write` and row-scope from `assertCanReadClass` → `assertCanWriteClass`, closing a latent grade-data leak to students/parents who held `tch-003:read`); rewrote the GradebookSnapshotWorker idempotency from at-most-once to at-least-once (read-only `IdempotencyService.isClaimed` on arrival, debounce-entry tracks contributing event-ids, `claim()` only fires AFTER a successful recompute, transient DB failures retry on the next event); and documented the temporary HR-employee identity mapping that columns named `*_employee_id` / `cls_grades.teacher_id` / `cls_lessons.teacher_id` / `cls_student_progress_notes.author_id` currently hold `iam_person.id` directly until the HR module ships — annotated in the migration SQL via `COMMENT ON COLUMN`. The ADR-057 event envelope is deferred to Cycle 3 (Communications) where multiple producers and consumers will arrive together; the grade-emit headers (`event-id`, `tenant-id`, `tenant-subdomain`) already carry the three fields the worker reads, so the migration is additive. Migrations `005_cls_lessons_and_assignments.sql` (7 tables) and `006_cls_submissions_and_grading.sql` (8 tables) add the full M21 Classroom schema — 15 tenant tables total. ADR-010 (gradebook snapshots are async-only) is enforced at the schema layout level: `cls_grades` and `cls_gradebook_snapshots` are physically separate tables with no FK or trigger linking them; the Step 5 grade write path was end-to-end-verified to never touch the snapshot table. AI / human boundary: `cls_ai_grading_jobs` and `cls_submission_question_grades.ai_*` columns store AI suggestions; `cls_grades` has zero `ai_*` columns and is written only by teacher action. 28 intra-tenant FKs across the two migrations; zero cross-schema FKs. Step 3 (`seed-classroom.ts`) seeds 1 grading scale, 5 assignment types, 18 per-class categories (Homework 30 / Assessments 50 / Participation 20), 12 assignments, 80 submissions, 62 grades (53 published), 41 gradebook snapshots, and 1 progress note for Maya — and adds TCH-002:read (Parent), TCH-004:read (Student), TCH-004:read+write (Teacher) to the role-permission map. Step 4 lands AssignmentService + CategoryService + their controllers — 7 endpoints under `tch-002:read/write` with the same row-level auth pattern as Cycle 1 (admin → all; teacher → assigned classes via `sis_class_teachers`; student → enrolled classes; parent → linked-child classes). Step 5 lands SubmissionService + GradeService + GradebookService + ProgressNoteService + 4 controllers — 12 new endpoints (3 `tch-002:*` + 9 `tch-003:*`). Submissions are idempotent upserts by `(assignment, student)`; resubmitting flips status back to `SUBMITTED`. Grade writes (single + batch + publish + unpublish + publish-all) emit `cls.grade.published` / `cls.grade.unpublished` via `KafkaProducerService` and never write to `cls_gradebook_snapshots`. Draft grades are filtered out of student / parent payloads (`rowToDto(row, includeDraftGrade)` flag — manager teacher / admin sees them; everyone else does not). Gradebook reads (`/classes/:id/gradebook`, `/students/:id/gradebook`) join roster ↔ snapshot per resolved term (default = current term). Progress notes upsert by `(class, student, term)` with persona-scoped reads (admins all; teachers their classes; students/parents only `is_*_visible=true` AND `published_at IS NOT NULL`). **Step 6 lands the first Kafka consumer in the system — `GradebookSnapshotWorker` (in `apps/api/src/classroom/`) — backed by `KafkaConsumerService` and `IdempotencyService` (in `apps/api/src/kafka/`). The worker subscribes to `cls.grade.published` and `cls.grade.unpublished` under consumer group `gradebook-snapshot-worker`, debounces 30 seconds per `(schoolId, classId, studentId)`, and recomputes `cls_gradebook_snapshots` using the exact weighted-average algorithm from `seed-classroom.ts` (per-category mean, weighted by category, renormalised over participating categories only). Idempotency is via `platform.platform_event_consumer_idempotency` keyed on `(consumer_group, event_id)`; redelivered events fail the `INSERT` (SQLSTATE 23505) and are dropped before they can reset the debounce. Tenant context is reconstructed at flush time from Kafka headers (`event-id`, `tenant-id`, `tenant-subdomain`) using `runWithTenantContextAsync`, so the worker reuses the same `TenantPrismaService` helpers as the request path. The grade emit (`grade.service.ts::tenantHeaders`) injects these three headers from `getCurrentTenant()` plus a fresh UUIDv7 — forward-compatible with the ADR-057 envelope that lands in Cycle 3.** **Step 7 lands the teacher Assignments UI: a `ClassTabs` shell at `/classes/:id/{attendance,assignments}` (Gradebook tab hidden until Step 8), an assignments list page with type filter / draft visibility for managers / soft-delete confirm, shared `AssignmentForm` for create + edit, and `CategoryWeightModal` that mirrors the API's PUT-by-name semantics with a live "weights must sum to 100" check. New `GET /assignment-types` endpoint exposes the school's type catalogue. Pre-existing lint / Suspense issues in the Cycle 1 dashboards + `/login` were fixed so the web app builds clean — these were transitive (only surfaced once Step 7 needed `next build` to succeed).** **Step 8 lands the teacher Grading UI: a spreadsheet-style gradebook grid at `/classes/:id/gradebook` with color-coded cells × inline editor × per-cell publish toggle × per-assignment "Publish all", a submissions queue at `/assignments/:id/submissions` sorted by grading priority (SUBMITTED first), a single-submission detail page at `/submissions/:id` with grade entry + feedback + publish controls, and a per-student `ProgressNoteModal` launched from the gradebook (auto-loads existing note for the (class, term) pair, upserts on save). The Gradebook tab is now visible across all `/classes/:id/*` routes. **Side fix: `grade.service.ts` now emits `cls.grade.published` on updates to already-published grades** (Step 5 emit gap — only draft↔published transitions were emitting before, so a re-grade of a published row left the snapshot stale). Both `upsertGrade` (single) and `batchGrade` paths now flag rows for emit if they end up published, regardless of prior state. The snapshot worker is idempotent so the redundant emit is harmless.** **Step 9 lands the student & parent grade views: a new student `StudentDashboard` (upcoming-assignment list + per-class average cards), a student `/assignments` inbox across all enrolled classes (filter by class / overdue / next-14-days), `/assignments/:id` student detail + submit form (idempotent resubmit, published-grade reveal), `/grades` per-class average list, `/grades/:classId` per-assignment breakdown, and the parent equivalents — a Grades section on each child card on `ParentDashboard`, `/children/:id/grades` per-child average list, and `/children/:id/grades/:classId` per-class breakdown (incl. parent-visible progress notes). Two new backend endpoints back this: `GET /students/me` (bootstrap the calling student's `studentId` without scanning the list) and `GET /students/:studentId/classes/:classId/grades` (per-(student, class) breakdown — assignments + own submission + own grade, only published rows for non-managers). Sidebar surfaces `Assignments` and `Grades` for STUDENT persona.** **Step 10 lands the Cycle 2 CAT at `docs/cycle2-cat-script.md` — a 9-step reproducible end-to-end walkthrough verified live against `tenant_demo`: teacher creates an assignment → student bootstraps via `/students/me` and submits → teacher grades draft → draft is hidden from the student → teacher publish-all emits `cls.grade.published` → 30-second debounce + idempotent recompute updates `cls_gradebook_snapshots` (worker log captured) → student sees grade via the new per-class endpoint → parent sees the same data through `sis_student_guardians` → 3 permission denials (student grading, parent submitting, teacher writing to a class they don't teach) plus a draft-after-unpublish bonus assertion. All assertions pass; demo state restored at end of run.**

See `docs/campusos-cycle1-implementation-plan.html`, `docs/campusos-cycle2-implementation-plan.html`, and `docs/campusos-cycle3-implementation-plan.html` for step-by-step plans; `HANDOFF-CYCLE1.md`, `HANDOFF-CYCLE2.md`, and `HANDOFF-CYCLE3.md` for current build state and known gaps; `REVIEW-CYCLE1-CHATGPT.md` / `REVIEW-CYCLE1-CLAUDE.md` / `REVIEW-CYCLE1-FIXES.md` for the Cycle 1 architecture review record; `REVIEW-CYCLE2-CHATGPT.md` for the Cycle 2 review + fixes-applied log; `REVIEW-CYCLE3-CHATGPT.md` for the Cycle 3 review (Round 1 REJECT → Round 2 APPROVED with the fix log inline).

- Cycle 0: Platform Foundation — COMPLETE
- Cycle 1: SIS Core + Attendance — COMPLETE (reviewed, fixes applied)
- Cycle 2: Classroom + Assignments + Grading — COMPLETE
- Cycle 3: Communications — COMPLETE + APPROVED at `592d366` (post-cycle review; see `REVIEW-CYCLE3-CHATGPT.md`). Vertical-slice CAT lives at `docs/cycle3-cat-script.md`; the closeout commit also ships 3 latent consumer-SQL fixes (`c.title` → `co.name`) and the 3 review fixes (Kafka consumer retry/DLQ, delivery worker PROCESSING state, announcement `isManager()` scope).

**Phase 2: Test and Refine (current — pending post-cycle review)**

- Walk through every workflow as each persona (teacher, parent, student, admin)

- Create UI design guide (`docs/ui-design-guide.md`) for future cycles
- Test edge cases and identify missing features within existing modules
- Fix issues and polish before expanding

**Phase 3: Expand — WAVE 1 CLOSED (all 8 cycles 0–8 + 6.1 polish post-review and approved). Wave 2 (Student Services) is next.**

School Admin and Platform Admin get all three tiers via `everyFunction` (447 each, +3). Effective access cache rebuilt for all 7 demo accounts; live verification via `unnest(permission_codes)` confirms each persona has exactly the expected `usr-001:*`codes (admins get read+write+admin; non-admins get read+write). Pre-existing cosmetic bug in`seed-iam.ts` closing log line (`var`shadowing of`tiers`from inner loop overwrites the outer array, prints 298 instead of 447) — not Step 3 scope, harmless. **Step 4 complete:** New`packages/database/src/seed-profile.ts`(idempotent, gated on whether Chen Family already has a HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD member; wired as`seed:profile`in package.json). Five sections in one script: A) Chen Family`platform_families`row updated with`address_line1='1234 Oak Street'`, Springfield IL 62701, home_phone, home_language='en', mailing_address_same=true; B) Chen members migrated PARENT→HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD (David, primary_contact preserved) and STUDENT→CHILD (Maya); C) iam_person personal fields on 6 accounts (David/Sarah/James/Linda/Marcus + Maya) with preferredName (Dave/Sarah/Jim/Linda/Marc/Maya), unique +1-217-555-01XX primary_phone, phone_type_primary='MOBILE' (exercises the Step 1 CHECK live), work_phone for the 4 staff, personal_email, preferred_language='en', profile_updated_at; Maya also gets date_of_birth='2011-03-15'; D) 15 sis_student_demographics rows with primary_language='English' (Maya additionally gets gender='Female') joined through sis_students.platform_student_id → platform_students.person_id to identify her; ON CONFLICT (student_id) DO NOTHING for row-level idempotency; E) David Chen's sis_guardians row gets employer='Chen Engineering LLC', employer_phone='+1-217-555-0177', occupation='Mechanical Engineer', work_address='100 Engineering Blvd, Springfield, IL 62701' (intentionally different physical address from home so the Employment vs Household tab split is visually distinct). Idempotent re-run logs `Chen Family already migrated to household roles. Skipping.`Live verification: 6 personas with preferred_name + phone_type_primary='MOBILE', 5 with personal_email (Maya intentionally null — students don't have personal email yet), 15 demographics rows (1 with gender), 1 sis_guardians row with employer. **Step 5 complete:** ProfileModule lands at`apps/api/src/profile/`(ProfileService + ProfileController + DTOs in`dto/profile.dto.ts`) and is wired into AppModule after AnnouncementsModule. 4 endpoints: `GET /profile/me`and`PATCH /profile/me`gated on`usr-001:read`/`write`; `GET /profile/:personId`and`PATCH /profile/:personId`gated on`usr-001:admin`. **Catalog correction caught during smoke:** the plan called for `iam-001:read/write`on the admin endpoints, but`IAM-001`does NOT exist in the catalogue (never has). Admin endpoints corrected to`usr-001:admin`, which Step 3 already granted exclusively to School Admin + Platform Admin via `everyFunction`. ProfileService composes platform iam_person + login email + household membership (always platform) with persona-conditional tenant data — `sis_student_demographics`for STUDENT (joined via sis_students.platform_student_id → platform_students.person_id),`sis_guardians` employment for GUARDIAN, dual-table emergency contact (`hr_emergency_contacts`keyed via`hr_employees.person_id`for STAFF;`sis_emergency_contacts`keyed via`sis_students.platform_student_id → platform_students.person_id`for STUDENT;`null`for everyone else — guardians have no emergency-contact schema home today, intentionally Phase 2 polish). PATCH allow-list excludes identity fields (firstName/lastName/dateOfBirth post-set) on the self-service path; admin path adds them. Service tx model: platform writes (iam_person.update) implicit Prisma tx, tenant writes (sis_student_demographics + sis_guardians + sis_emergency_contacts/hr_emergency_contacts) all run inside one`executeInTenantTransaction`callback for atomicity, the two transactions are sequential not nested. **Class-hoisting bug caught during build:**`UpdateEmergencyContactDto`was declared after`UpdateMyProfileDto`even though the latter's`@ApiPropertyOptional({ type: () => UpdateEmergencyContactDto })`decorator evaluates eagerly at class-definition time; reordered to declare it first. Live smoke (8 scenarios on tenant_demo): David parent GET own returns full shape with HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD household + Chen Engineering employment; Maya student GET own returns demographics inline (gender Female, primaryLanguage English, dateOfBirth 2011-03-15); Jim teacher GET own returns STAFF without household; David PATCH preferredName + secondaryPhone with phone_type='HOME' (CHECK fires and passes); David PATCH firstName 400 by ValidationPipe forbidNonWhitelisted; Jim PATCH emergencyContact lands in hr_emergency_contacts with is_primary=true and source='EMPLOYEE'; Sarah admin PATCH Maya firstName via /profile/:personId works on usr-001:admin; David PATCH /profile/:Sarah's personId 403 INSUFFICIENT_PERMISSIONS. Cleanup restores tenant to post-Step-4 seed state. **Step 6 complete:** HouseholdsModule lands at`apps/api/src/households/`with 6 endpoints (the plan said 5; added`GET /households/:id`for arbitrary-by-id reads with row-scope to member-or-admin). Self-service household editing gated on`usr-001:write`plus service-layer`assertCanEditHousehold(familyId, actor)`— short-circuits true if`actor.isSchoolAdmin`OR caller has`usr-001:admin`, otherwise reads `platform_family_members`for the (family, person) pair and accepts HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD or SPOUSE only. **Transactional discipline:** every state-change opens a regular`prisma.$transaction` (NOT `executeInTenantTransaction` — household tables live in the platform schema), takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on `platform_families.id`, runs the membership gate inside the same tx, then mutates. Atomic primary-contact promotion: when `isPrimaryContact=true` lands, the service explicitly clears any existing primary in the same tx so the partial UNIQUE INDEX `(family_id) WHERE is_primary_contact=true` (Step 1) never fires; only the final UPDATE flips the new primary on. Refuses last-HEAD demotion + self-eviction without admin. Member-side mutations emit `iam.household.member_changed` via `KafkaProducerService.emit({sourceModule:'iam'})`; address-only PATCH does NOT emit. TypeScript fix during build: `tx` inside `$transaction`is`Prisma.TransactionClient`, not full `PrismaClient`. Live smoke (10 scenarios on tenant_demo): David parent GET /my canEdit=true; Maya GET /my canEdit=false; Maya PATCH 403 "Only the head of household or spouse..."; David PATCH addressLine1 200; David adds Sarah as SPOUSE 200; David promotes Sarah to primary → David's primary atomically cleared in same tx, Sarah is now sole primary; David tries to demote himself (last HEAD) 400; David self-eviction 400; David removes test SPOUSE Sarah 200; Sarah admin PATCH any household 200 (admin override). 4 envelopes captured live on `dev.iam.household.member_changed`with full ADR-057 shape (event_type, source_module='iam', tenant_id, fresh event_id/correlation_id UUIDv7s, event_version=1; payloads cover ADDED / UPDATED / REMOVED / UPDATED). Cleanup restores tenant to post-Step-4 seed state. **Step 7 complete:** Profile UI lands at`apps/web/src/app/(app)/profile/page.tsx`(self-service, 987 B) +`apps/web/src/app/(app)/profile/[personId]/page.tsx`(admin, 1.23 kB) with shared 6-tab module`apps/web/src/components/profile/ProfileTabs.tsx`(Personal Info / My Household / Emergency Contact / Demographics for STUDENT / Employment for GUARDIAN / Account). Tabs are persona-conditional via`profileTabs(personType)`from new`apps/web/src/lib/profile-format.ts`(also exports`profileCompleteness(profile)`0-100% formula based on required fields like primary_phone/first_name/last_name/household-role weight 1.0 + recommended fields like preferred_name/emergency-contact/employer/primary_language weight 0.5; and PHONE_TYPES + HOUSEHOLD_ROLES + label maps). New`apps/web/src/hooks/use-profile.ts`with 9 hooks: useMyProfile / useProfile(personId) / useUpdateMyProfile / useUpdateProfile(personId) / useMyHousehold / useHousehold(id) / useUpdateHousehold(id) / useAddHouseholdMember(id) / useUpdateHouseholdMember(id, memberId) / useRemoveHouseholdMember(id). 30s staleTime on reads. 13 new DTO interfaces in`apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`. Avatar dropdown in `TopBar.tsx`gains "My Profile" link above "Sign out" for any user with`usr-001:read`(= every persona). No new launchpad tile per the design principle ("launchpad, not dashboard"). Household tab uses`canEdit`from the API to drive editability; primary-contact promotion via inline per-member actions. Add-member modal accepts raw UUID for now (directory picker is future polish). Demographics tab greys out admin-only fields (gender, ethnicity, etc.) for self-service STUDENT view; admin`/profile/[personId]`lets admins edit them. Two build issues caught: Tailwind shorthand classes`.input`/`.btn-primary`/`.btn-secondary`don't exist in`globals.css`(project uses explicit class strings throughout) — swapped to the`/apply/new`form pattern; unused`useUpdateHouseholdMember`alias left in`HouseholdEditor`— removed. Both APIs underlying the routes verified live for parent + student personas (David`GET /profile/me`returns GUARDIAN+employment+household.role=HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD; Maya`GET /profile/me`returns STUDENT+demographics+household.role=CHILD; David`GET /households/my`returns canEdit=true; Maya`GET /households/my`returns canEdit=false). Production build's static prerender succeeded for`/profile`confirming no runtime errors at compile time. Full browser smoke (click-through save buttons, modal open/close, primary-contact promotion) is the Step 8 CAT's concern. **Transactional convention (clarification):** Household endpoints (Step 6) write only to platform-schema tables and so use a regular Prisma`$transaction`, NOT `executeInTenantTransaction`— the tenant`search_path`is irrelevant for platform writes. Profile endpoints (Step 5) compose: a single`PATCH /profile/me`may write`iam_person`(platform tx) AND`sis_guardians`/`sis_emergency_contacts`/`sis_student_demographics`(tenant tx) as separate transactions in order. **Step 8 complete:** Vertical-slice CAT lands at`docs/cycle6.1-cat-script.md`— schema preamble (12 checks all green: 22 iam_person columns + 20 platform_families columns + 2 platform_family_members new cols + 10 MemberRole enum values + partial UNIQUE on is_primary_contact + 10 sis_student_demographics columns + 4 sis_guardians employment columns + 3 USR-001 perm rows + 447 catalog total + Chen Family seed shape + 15 demographics rows + 6 personas with preferred_name) + 11 plan scenarios all pass on`tenant_demo`(S1 GUARDIAN composition; S2 phone_type CHECK passes on HOME; S3 ValidationPipe forbidNonWhitelisted on firstName; S4 HEAD_OF_HOUSEHOLD address PATCH; S5 CHILD canEdit=false + 403; S6 demographics self-service/admin-only split; S7 dual-table emergency contact lands in hr_emergency_contacts source=EMPLOYEE; S8 admin override on usr-001:admin; S9 full member lifecycle ADD→promote→demote-self refused→REMOVE→primary-restore with 4 envelopes captured; S10 permission denials parent/teacher 403, student row-scope 404; S11 web build static prerender succeeds for both /profile + /profile/[personId]). 4 ADR-057 envelopes captured live on`dev.iam.household.member_changed`with`source_module='iam'`+ tenant_id populated + fresh UUIDv7 event_id/correlation_id + event_version=1; payloads cover ADDED / UPDATED-promote / REMOVED / UPDATED-restore. Cleanup restores`tenant_demo`to post-Step-4 seed state. **Cycle 6.1 ships clean to the post-cycle architecture review.** Git tag pattern:`cycle6.1-complete`after this commit + CI green;`cycle6.1-approved`after the post-cycle architecture review verdict. Review prompt scaffold lives at`REVIEW-CYCLE6.1-CHATGPT.md`. **REVIEW-CYCLE6.1 (2026-04-29):** Round 1 at `e72525e`returned **REJECT** with 3 BLOCKING + 4 DEVIATION findings, all VALID. Fix commit lands all 7 with live verification: (BLOCKING 1)`assertTargetInCurrentTenant(personId)`added to ProfileService — admin profile endpoints`GET/PATCH /profile/:personId`now require the target person to have a`sis_students`/`sis_guardians`/`hr_employees`row in the calling tenant; school admin from school A can no longer read or edit a person from school B by guessing the UUID; verified live (Sarah GETs Maya 200, Sarah GETs admin@ 404, Sarah PATCHes admin@ 404). (BLOCKING 2)`HouseholdsService.hasAdmin(actor, familyId)`now ANDs the IAM permission with`householdAffiliatedWithCurrentTenant(familyId)`— at least one household member must have a`sis\*\*`/`hr_employees`row in the calling tenant; school admin from school A can no longer mutate school B's household via platform_families UUID; verified live (Sarah PATCHes Chen Family 200, Sarah GETs/PATCHes/POSTs phantom family 404 across the board). Companion 404-leak tightening:`assertCanEditHousehold`now returns 404 (not 403) for non-member non-admin actors, matching BLOCKING 2's "don't leak existence" intent. (BLOCKING 3)`upsertEmergencyContact` for STAFF + STUDENT now locks the parent row (`hr_employees`/`sis_students`) with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`before any reads/writes; partial UNIQUE INDEX`(employee_id) WHERE is_primary=true`23505 leaks now caught by`isUniqueViolation`helper and translated to 409; verified live with 5 parallel PATCHes serialising cleanly (5×200, exactly 1 hr_emergency_contacts row remaining, is_primary=true). (DEVIATION 4)`previousNames`DTO gains`@MinLength(1, { each: true })`+`@MaxLength(100, { each: true })`so`[''']`is rejected with 400. (DEVIATION 5)`apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`adds`LegacyHouseholdRole`(PARENT/GUARDIAN/STUDENT) +`AnyHouseholdRole`union;`HOUSEHOLD_ROLE_LABELS`extended to all 10 enum values with "(legacy)" suffix on the 3 deprecated ones so a leaked legacy row never renders as`undefined`. (DEVIATION 6) `applyUpdate`moved iam_person.update INTO the existing`executeInTenantTransaction`callback — both schemas now write atomically in a single tx, eliminating the partial-save failure mode where iam_person committed but tenant tx failed; static verification: 0`this.platform.iamPerson.update`call sites in applyUpdate, 1`tx.iamPerson.update`inside the tenant tx. (DEVIATION 7)`isUniqueViolation(err)`helper in both ProfileService + HouseholdsService checks`err.code === 'P2010'`OR`err.meta?.code === '23505'`OR message regex — the SQLSTATE check is driver-version-stable. Live smoke confirms all 7 fixes;`pnpm format:check`clean;`pnpm --filter @campusos/api build` clean.
- Cycle 7: Tasks & Approval Workflows — **COMPLETE, awaiting post-cycle architecture review.** All 10 steps shipped. Migrations `026` + `027` (M1 + M2 schemas, 108 → 120 tenant base tables). 8 auto-task rules + 3 workflow templates seeded. TaskWorker (sole writer to tsk_tasks per ADR-011), WorkflowEngineService (sole writer to wsk_approval_requests + wsk_approval_steps per ADR-012), 23 endpoints (5 task + 4 ack + 7 approval + 2 template + leave migration). 7 web routes (3 tasks + 4 approvals/admin). LeaveService migrated onto the engine with backward-compatible direct-PATCH override. Vertical-slice CAT at `docs/cycle7-cat-script.md` walks 10 scenarios end-to-end on tenant_demo with full cleanup. Tagged `cycle7-complete`. See `HANDOFF-CYCLE7.md` for full per-step detail.
- Cycle 8: Service Tickets — **COMPLETE + APPROVED + FOLLOW-UP APPROVED. Round 1 of REVIEW-CYCLE8-CHATGPT (against `cycle8-complete` at `fa0797c`) returned "Approved with major follow-ups" — no BLOCKING items, 5 major follow-ups split into 3 clean bug fixes (landed in `c424288`) + 2 architectural decisions carried to Phase 2 backlog. Round 2 (follow-up review against `c424288` / `cycle8-approved`) returned "Approved" — final gate decision; reviewer confirmed all three code-level follow-ups (F2 resolver self-notification, F3 ProblemService.patch validation, F4 locationId soft-ref validation) are properly addressed; F1 (IT-001 permission rename) + F5 (per-parent category UNIQUE) appropriately remain as Phase 2 backlog. All 10 steps done; CAT verified live; tagged `cycle8-complete` at `fa0797c`, `cycle8-approved` at `c424288`. Wave 1 is closed; the platform ships the complete core operational stack (SIS + Classroom + Communications + HR + Scheduling + Enrollment + Payments + Profile/Household + Tasks + Approvals + Service Tickets) and is ready for Wave 2 (Student Services — Behaviour, Health, Counselling, Library, Athletics & Clubs). REVIEW-CYCLE8 fixes (2026-05-04): F2 resolver-self-notification suppression (`resolvedByAccountId` added to resolved payload + consumer compares against `requesterId`); F3 `ProblemService.patch` locked-row validation rejecting bare KNOWN_ERROR / assignee-vendor mutex / bogus FK targets; F4 `TicketService.create` validates `locationId` against `sch_rooms` before insert. F1 (IT-001 permission rename) + F5 (per-parent category UNIQUE) deferred to Phase 2 backlog. See `REVIEW-CYCLE8-CHATGPT.md`.** Step 1 migration `028_tkt_categories_sla_vendors.sql` adds 4 configuration base tables. Step 2 migration `029_tkt_tickets_and_activity.sql` adds 7 operational base tables (tickets with 3 multi-column CHECKs — assignee/vendor mutex + vendor pair lockstep + resolved/closed lifecycle; tkt_ticket_activity IMMUTABLE audit per ADR-010; problems + problem_tickets link). 18 new intra-tenant FKs across both schema steps, 0 cross-schema FKs, tenant logical base table count 121 → 125 → **132**. Step 3 ships `seed-tickets.ts` (idempotent, gated on tkt_categories) + 7 sections seeded on tenant_demo: 3 categories, 11 subcategories (Sarah Mitchell as default_assignee on IT/Hardware + 'SCHOOL_ADMIN' role on Facilities/Electrical), 12 SLA policies (CRITICAL 1h/4h, HIGH 2h/8h, MEDIUM 4h/24h, LOW 8h/72h), 2 vendors (1 preferred), 5 sample tickets across all 5 status states (OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/RESOLVED/VENDOR_ASSIGNED/CLOSED), 3 comments (1 internal), 8 activity rows, 1 problem linking T1+T3, 1 auto-task rule on tkt.ticket.assigned (HIGH/ADMINISTRATIVE/24h offset). `seed-iam.ts` extends Teacher (38→40) and Staff (18→20) with IT-001:read+write; School Admin / Platform Admin already hold IT-001:admin + FAC-001:admin via everyFunction. FAC-001 read/write deliberately not extended to non-admin staff this cycle — IT-001 is the umbrella code Step 4's TicketService will gate on for all ticket categories. Step 4 ships 4 services + 4 controllers + 18 endpoints + 3 Kafka emits with auto-assignment chain + SLA auto-link + locked-row state machine. Step 5 adds 3 more services (ActivityService + CommentService + ProblemService) + 9 more endpoints, taking the total to **27**. ActivityService hoists Step 4's private `recordActivity()` into a shared writer; CommentService implements visibility filter (admin/assignee see all, requester sees public-only, non-participant 404) with first-staff-comment SLA-stop bump on `first_response_at` and `tkt.ticket.commented` Kafka emit; ProblemService ships 6 admin-only endpoints including the batch-resolve keystone that locks problem + linked active tickets `FOR UPDATE OF t` in one tx, flips problem to RESOLVED with root_cause/resolution/workaround populated, batch-flips matching tickets to RESOLVED + activity rows + per-ticket `tkt.ticket.resolved` emits with `resolvedViaProblemId`. Step 5 live smoke (12 scenarios on tenant_demo) all pass: activity reads cross-persona, comment visibility model + first_response_at bump + Kafka envelope, problem batch-resolve flips T1+T2 leaving already-RESOLVED T3 untouched, 4 permission denials. One iteration issue caught: array DTO fields (`LinkTicketsDto.ticketIds`, `CreateProblemDto.ticketIds`) need `@IsArray()` + `@IsUUID('all', { each: true })` decorators or the global `forbidNonWhitelisted` ValidationPipe silently drops them. Build clean, all 27 ticket routes mapped on boot. Step 6 wires 2 new Kafka consumers — `TicketNotificationConsumer` (notifications module, all 4 `tkt.ticket.*` topics, fan-out matrix per topic with internal-comment-stays-staff-side rule) and `TicketTaskCompletionConsumer` (tasks module, on `tkt.ticket.resolved`, runs UPDATE … RETURNING and emits `task.completed` per flipped auto-task, closing the Step 3 seed → Step 4 emit → DONE-cascade loop). Live verified on `tenant_demo` end-to-end including Redis IN_APP delivery for both requester + assignee. Step 7 ships the helpdesk staff UI — `Helpdesk` launchpad tile gated on `it-001:read` + 3 routes (`/helpdesk`, `/helpdesk/new`, `/helpdesk/[id]`) + 13 React Query hooks + `tickets-format.ts` with `slaUrgency` 4-state helper + LifebuoyIcon + 4 new `ticket.*` notification descriptors in NotificationBell + helpdesk badge counter wired into use-app-badges. Build sizes 7.89/9.96/8.86 kB. Step 8 ships the helpdesk admin UI (`/helpdesk/admin/{queue,sla,categories,vendors}`) — 4 admin-only routes + 7 new admin mutation hooks taking the cycle hook count to 20. Queue with multi-filter + per-row Assign/Vendor modals + SLA-urgency-tinted breached rows; SLA dashboard with stat cards + by-priority histogram + per-category breakdown; tree editor with employee-XOR-role auto-assignment mutex client-side; vendor CRUD with full contact info form. No API changes — also sits on the 27-endpoint surface. Step 9 ships problem management UI (`/helpdesk/admin/problems` list + detail) — 6 new problem hooks (cycle total 26) + Resolve modal with batch-flip preview + Link Tickets modal with multi-select + Create-problem-from-ticket button on the staff detail page that pre-populates categoryId and seeds the link. 9 routes total now (3 staff + 4 admin + 2 problems). No API changes — sits on the 6 problem endpoints from Step 5. Step 10 ships `docs/cycle8-cat-script.md` — 10-check schema preamble + 10 plan scenarios verified live on `tenant_demo` (auto-assign + SLA link + Cycle 7 TaskWorker + DONE-cascade + visibility filter + vendor assign + problem batch-resolve + reopen + 6 permission denials all green). All 10 steps of Cycle 8 done. **Cycle 8 is the final cycle of Wave 1** — platform now ready for Wave 2 (Student Services). See `HANDOFF-CYCLE8.md` and `docs/cycle8-cat-script.md`. Plan at `docs/campusos-cycle8-implementation-plan.html`; handoff at `HANDOFF-CYCLE8.md`.
- Each cycle follows validated patterns from Phase 1
- UI matches the design guide established in Phase 2

**Wave 2: Student Services — IN PROGRESS.** With Wave 1 closed (`cycle8-approved` at `c424288`, follow-up review APPROVED), the platform is now executing Wave 2. Wave 2 covers Behaviour (Cycle 9 — APPROVED), Health (Cycle 10 — APPROVED), Counselling (Cycle 11 — IN PROGRESS), Library, and Athletics & Clubs. Each cycle follows the validated Wave 1 pattern: implementation plan HTML in `docs/`, per-cycle handoff (`HANDOFF-CYCLE9.md` etc.), 10 numbered steps ending in a vertical-slice CAT, post-cycle architecture review with verdict file.

- **Cycle 12: Library — IN PROGRESS — backend phase complete (Steps 1–7).** Cycle 12 ships the M24 Library module — 14 of the 20 ERD tables in scope (catalogue + locations + copies in Step 1; circulation policies + checkouts + holds + fines in Step 2; reading programmes + reading lists + reviews in Step 3; recommendations / class-set checkouts / interlibrary loans / catalogue import / scan sessions / Library Space deferred). Cycle 12 is the **first entirely new module prefix** (`lib_*`) since Cycle 10's `hlth_*`, the **second-to-last cycle of Wave 2 Student Services** (Cycle 13 Athletics & Clubs closes the wave), and ships the **second student-input surface in CampusOS** after Cycle 11.1 (students log reading entries + write book reviews). **Step 1 lands `043_lib_catalogue.sql`** with 3 logical base tables — `lib_locations` (6-value location*type CHECK, UNIQUE(school_id, name), is_active for soft deactivation), `lib_catalogue_items` (bibliographic record with **GIN INDEX on `to_tsvector('english', title || ' ' || COALESCE(author, ''))`** backing the Step 5 catalogue search bar with `ts_rank` + `plainto_tsquery`; INDEX on isbn for direct ISBN lookup), `lib_catalogue_copies` (UNIQUE barcode — the scannable spine identifier for the Step 5 `GET /library/copies/barcode/:barcode` keystone; 5-value `condition` CHECK; 7-value `location_status` CHECK; `is_available` denormalised so the catalogue list can compute available-copies count without joining lib_checkouts; partial INDEX on `(catalogue_item_id, is_available)`). 2 new intra-tenant FKs (CASCADE on copy → item, SET NULL on copy → location); 0 cross-schema FKs. **Step 2 lands `044_lib_circulation.sql`** with 4 more logical base tables — `lib_checkout_policies` (UNIQUE(school, patron_type) per-school per-patron-type policy with non-negative CHECKs), `lib_checkouts` (one row per (copy, patron) borrow event; `copy_id` FK NO ACTION financial-audit guard; soft `patron_id` covering students + staff via the ADR-055 person model; 4-value status CHECK + **multi-column `returned_chk` keystone** keeping returned_at in lockstep across the 4 lifecycle phases; partial INDEXes for active + overdue hot paths), `lib_holds` (5-value status CHECK + **multi-column `pending_chk`** + **partial INDEX (item, placed_at) WHERE status='PENDING'** keystone for the Step 6 HoldService.fulfillNext oldest-first pickup), `lib_fines` (3-value type/status CHECKs + soft invoice_id ref to pay_invoices for future Cycle 6 payment integration consumer; emits `lib.fine.issued` on INSERT). 3 new intra-tenant FKs (NO ACTION × 2 + CASCADE × 1). **Step 3 lands `045_lib_reading_reviews.sql`** completing the schema phase — 7 logical base tables: `lib_reading_programmes` (4-value `target_audience_type` CHECK + soft polymorphic target_id), `lib_reading_logs` (**THE SECOND STUDENT-INPUT SURFACE in CampusOS after Cycle 11.1 wellbeing**; student_id CASCADE / catalogue_item_id NO ACTION; rating 1-5 + pages non-negative CHECKs), `lib_programme_progress` (UNIQUE(programme, student) running totals), `lib_programme_completions` (NO ACTION on programme_id preserving the audit; CASCADE on student_id; awarded_by SET NULL; UNIQUE(programme, student)), `lib_reading_lists` (5-value `list_type` CHECK + **multi-column `published_chk` lockstep keystone** + **UNIQUE(school, name, COALESCE(academic_year_id, sentinel))** — the COALESCE-sentinel pattern matching Cycle 5 sch_periods + Cycle 6 enr_intake_capacities), `lib_reading_list_items` (4-value `item_type` CHECK + UNIQUE(list, item) + sort_order non-negative), `lib_reviews` (UNIQUE(item, student) one review per student per item; rating 1-5 NOT NULL; is_approved=false the librarian soft-hide path). 15 new intra-tenant FKs (CASCADE × 6 / NO ACTION × 8 / SET NULL × 1). 0 cross-schema FKs across all three steps. **Tenant logical base table count: 175 → 178 → 182 → 189** (3 + 4 + 7). \*\*Cycle 12 schema phase complete: 14 lib*\* tables across 3 migrations, 20 intra-tenant FKs (2 + 3 + 15).** Step 1 17-assertion smoke green; Step 2 30-assertion smoke green; **Step 3 35-assertion smoke green** (every CHECK / UNIQUE / FK rejection / multi-column lockstep on published_chk / COALESCE-sentinel UNIQUE both directions / CASCADE chains both directions / NO ACTION refusals / SET NULL on awarded_by / catalog readout confirming the 15-FK distribution exactly). Migrations renumbered from the plan's 041/042/043 because those slots were taken by post-Cycle-11.1 fix migrations; Cycle 12 uses 043 / 044 / 045. Splitter trap caught + fixed pre-provision on every step (Step 1: 0 stray; Step 2: 1 stray inside COMMENT ON TABLE; Step 3: 2 strays inside lib_reviews block-comment description); **seventeenth migration in a row to clear the trap on first provision attempt after audit\*\* (Cycles 4–12 unbroken streak — the audit-then-provision discipline is the load-bearing rule). Idempotent re-provision verified for all three steps on both `tenant_demo` and `tenant_test`. Plan at `docs/campusos-cycle12-implementation-plan.html`; handoff at `HANDOFF-CYCLE12.md`. (Steps 4–10 — seed + LIB-001..003 IAM grants, NestJS catalogue + circulation + reading-and-reviews modules with `lib.fine.issued` Kafka emit, librarian + student library UI, vertical-slice CAT — remain to ship.)
- **Cycle 11.1: Wellbeing Check-Ins — COMPLETE + APPROVED at `6d2b04c`.** Round 3 of REVIEW-CYCLE11.1-CHATGPT returned APPROVED (final gate). Round 1 was misdirected (reviewer pulled pre-`a5abe4e` `main` and reviewed Phase 2 Parent Polish state instead — 2 BLOCKINGs verified spurious; 2 real Phase 2 MAJORs (child-link dedup) landed in `b58e591`).
